# Escapement (chirpers11): the sound bank

Clicky minimal techno: few sounds, each one designed. Add every new patch, kit, bus and recipe to the table as you make it: name, what it is, what it was built from, where it lives. New names only: released albums render through the library, so never edit a patch another record uses (`tools/libguard.py`). Song-local patches (in a song's own `patches:`) are free; say when one is worth banking.

## The click family

| sound | what it is | built from | where it lives | notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `gt-kick` (chirper-zero, t1 v1) | the bass drum: short, dry, resting on G1 | the engine's `machine` kick: `decay 280ms, click 0.65, drive 0.35, punch 22, punch_time 34ms`; hp 28 | song-local, Give or Take | the kick's own body is under 60 Hz: the mix reads 8 to 10 dB short at 70-135 Hz beside the album's envelope |
| `gt-tick` (chirper-zero, t1 v1) | the grid click: one dry rim, on the off-beats | `machine` rim, `decay 35ms`, played on G5 | song-local, Give or Take | the rim follows the note: on D6 it put 10 dB on the 1.2 kHz band, on G5 it does not |
| `gt-hat` (chirper-zero, t1 v1) | the sixteenth click: 14 ms of white noise between 6 and 12.5 kHz | `white` through hp 6000, a peak at 8.5 kHz, lp 12500; amp `d 14ms, curve 5` | song-local, Give or Take | so short that it needs a fader far over a hat's to be heard (0 dB here, and it still averages 16.5 dB under the mix) |
| `gt-tock` (chirper-zero, t1 v1) | a wooden bar, struck and gone, whose pitch follows a lane | the `modal` layer on the glock's modes (after Common Measure's `cm-clave`): `bright 0.9, damp 0.25, decay 160ms`; `pitch` moved by `auto.spd` (semitones) | song-local, Give or Take | the first patch here whose tuning is a function of time |
| `gt-clap` (chirper-zero, t1 v1) | a short clap on two and four | `machine` clap, `bursts 3, decay 140ms` | song-local, Give or Take | |
| `gt-stab` (chirper-zero, t1 v1) | a dry dub chord that goes sharp and flat with its deck | the library's house chord (three saws and a square, a short filter envelope) without its own effects; `pitch` moved by `auto.spd` | song-local, Give or Take | its echo is the bus `dub` (a dotted eighth, feedback 0.45, hp 350, a hall) |
| `gt-knock` (chirper-zero, t1 v2) | deck B's own bass drum: the incoming record with its bass out, and in | `machine` kick (`decay 220ms, click 0.8, drive 0.3, punch 20`), a high-pass whose cutoff rides the lane `low` (32 Hz to 315 Hz), pitch on `auto.spd`; a `gain` fx on the same lane turns what is left up 7 dB while the bass is out | song-local, Give or Take | the bass swap of a DJ mix as two lanes: 17.5 dB more under 120 Hz when it is in. Without the lift its strokes stood 14 dB under the mix |
| `gt-meet` (chirper-zero, t1 v4) | Least Common's ping, quoted a track early: what two things make when they meet | chirper-one's `lc-ping` rebuilt under this song's name: `modal` `vibes`, `decay 650ms`, `bright 0.55`, `strike 0.3`, `amp d 900ms` | song-local, Give or Take | at -14 dB, room -10 dB: its strokes stand 9.0 dB under the mix in "locked", beside the tock (9.8) and the grid click (7.4) |
| `gt-roll` (chirper-zero, t1 v5) | a loop of a drum: `gt-knock` with its bass out (a fixed high-pass at 315 Hz), let go the instant its note ends (`r 1ms`), so a note as long as the loop IS the loop: the first instant of the drum, over and over | the machine kick (`decay 220ms`, `click 0.8`), `hp 315`, `amp a 0.1ms s 1 r 1ms` | song-local, Give or Take | at +3 dB it is within 4.2 dB of the whole mix in every loop of Zeno's bar (16.5 to 2116 turns a second) |

For a thing struck ON the beat use phase 0.25 and `rate: 1/2` (down on the off-beat) | bench, one stroke let go, alone: 12 dB under `rt-metal`'s ring 240 and 720 ms after the stroke, level with it at 0 and 480 ms, the same highest sample. At 9 (18 dB dips) the ring is 9 dB less on average. Two identical `modal` layers in one voice are NOT a controllable doublet: each gets its own few cents (0 to 2.8 beats a second at 1.4 kHz, by stroke and by seed) |
| `rt-drum-disc` (chirper-one, t4 v18) | the weight with the metal's disc under its head: struck on the beat, its ring is 12 dB down on every off-beat and back on the beat; replaces `rt-drum` in Ring True | `rt-drum`'s two layers; the head's (`modal kettle`) `level` is 6 dB lower and moved 6 dB by `lfo.motor`: `synth.lfo.motor = {wave: sine, rate: 1/4, phase: 0.25, retrig: true}`; the felt thud's layer is untouched | a bass drum whose boom gets out of the off-beat by itself (no sidechain); with `rt-motor` on a thing struck off the beat the two rings alternate. The stroke and a stopped stroke are unchanged | bench, one stroke let go (ring 2.96 s), alone, 40 to 200 Hz: 11 to 12 dB under `rt-drum`'s ring 240 and 720 ms after the stroke, level with it at 480 and 960 ms; the same highest sample. Finished, it costs the low band 1 to 2.4 dB in the sections where the drum rings. NOT on a `noise` layer: the same LFO on the air's breath layers raised them 0.9 dB and moved them 0.5 dB where 3 was written |
| `rt-drum-heft` (chirper-one, t4 v19) | the weight with the disc under its head (`rt-drum-disc`) and the head 3 dB up under it: open on the beat its ring is 3 dB over `rt-drum`'s, shut on the off-beat 9 dB under; replaces `rt-drum-disc` in Ring True | `rt-drum-disc` with the head's (`modal kettle`) `level` value -9 dB for -12 dB (still moved 6 dB by `lfo.motor`, a turn a beat); the felt thud's layer is untouched | one stroke let go, alone: 3 dB more from 40 to 200 Hz in every 60 ms of its ring; highest sample 2.2 dB hotter; a stopped stroke as it was (0.2 dB). In the finished song 40 to 120 Hz is 0.3 to 0.5 dB up where the drum is stopped and 0.9 to 1.5 dB up where it is let go (bars 113 to 136) | a ring that a disc turns down is a quieter ring: give the head back what the disc takes (half the dip), and read the off-beat against the beat again: here the dip came out 0.4 to 0.7 dB deeper, not shallower |
| `rt-true` (chirper-one, t4 v21) | the metal that rings true: `rt-motor`'s bar and disc with the engine's stroke-to-stroke wander off, so that two of them a set number of Hz apart breathe at that rate; replaces `rt-motor` in Ring True (every stroke of the metal, and its hall's copy) | `rt-motor` with `vary: 0` on its `modal glock` layer; nothing else | a pair of struck bars tuned apart on purpose (a gamelan's): the second on its own track, same fader and place, its pitch written in Hz (the note's plus the gap), each 6 dB under the single stroke | bench, eight strokes on F6: `rt-motor` 1395.4 to 1400.6 Hz, `rt-true` 1396.9 to 1397.6; a pair 8.33 Hz apart: the first minimum 54 to 81 ms after the stroke (written 60), the later ones within 12 ms of 180, 300, 420, 540 and 660 on all eight (`rt-motor`: gaps of 4.9 to 12.3 Hz); the metal alone, a sixteenth apart: 11 to 15 dB down at 60, 180, 300, 420 ms and within 2 dB of one bar at 120, 240, 360, 480; the stroke's highest sample the single bar's to 0.5 dB. In the finished song only the first dip is shown (8 to 17 dB under v20, 60 ms after the stroke) |
| `rt-glass` and the track `glass` (chirper-one, t4 v24) | a glass sung by a finger on its rim: the one thing in a kit of struck things that sounds only while it is held | three sines: the note, a twin 4 cents over at -8 dB (two near modes beat), a partial 14.57 semitones up at -20 dB; amp a 500 ms, s 1, r 1 s; `play: {gate: 1, vel_sens: 0.3}`; the first two levels on `lfo.rub` (sine, rate `3/8`, retrig, 6 dB): the finger's turn, two to three beats; fader -28 dB, pan 0.2, room -14 dB; notes -3 and -6 dB as they climb | one held note a section under a wind-down of clicks, under the strokes and over the gaps | finished: 3.6 to 5.7 dB under the music in a full row, 9 to 15 over its quietest 100 ms; the master lifts it 1.5 to 4 dB against the strokes and dips it 5.6 dB at every drum stroke; a rub once a beat at 3 dB could not be told from that dip; its side moves by note in the room (2.8 dB right to 0.4 left) |
| `rt-rasp` and the track `rasp` (chirper-one, t4 v25) | a notched stick, scraped: the one thing in the kit whose stroke is a row of clicks and not a click | a modal bar of 6 ms (bright 0.9, strike 0.8) with 2 ms of white noise on it at -18 dB (`env.burst`); amp a 0.1 ms, s 1, r 20 ms; `play: {event_stable: true, vel_sens: 0.6}`; fader -12 dB, pan -0.45, room -14 dB; the notches 8 dB under the row's last click, a last click with no drum under it 6 dB down, and a dB more on the row for each notch | rows of one to eight clicks a sixty-fourth apart, each a step of the five notes under the next, the last on beat 3 of the bar; the number of notches counts the things that ring | even to 0.1 dB click to click on the bench (noise alone through a narrow band was not: -11 to -23 dBFS for the same click); this master lifts a click between the drum's strokes 15.5 dB where one thing rings and 9 to 12 at the peak, one on the drum 8 to 10 (6 at the peak), one on a beat the drum has left about 14: at one fader the notches were the loudest thing in the song |
| `rt-tine` and the track `comb` (chirper-one, t4 v26) | the tenth thing, the only one that is plucked: a comb of steel tongues, one for each thing; the song's tune | three layers: a sine; a sine at `ratio` 6.27 (a clamped bar's second mode) on a level that falls 68 dB in 200 ms; 1.5 ms of white noise (the nail); amp to nothing in 450 ms; fader -14 dB, centre, room -14 dB; a trim for each of its 24 places (-8.2 to +6.1 dB) and up to 9 dB off for a thing that rings little | Bb3 F4 D4 C4 G4 Bb4 F3 in the order the things are let go; 392 notes, bars 37 to 166; the phrase begins on sixteenth 6 | finished (150 to 550 Hz): the 24 places -11.0 to -12.8 dBFS; raw and alone a note is -15 to -33 by its trim: this master lifts it 1.6 to 16.3 dB by place |
| `rt-seeds` and the track `gourd` (chirper-one, t4 v28) | the eleventh thing, the only one that is shaken: a gourd of dry seeds; the song's one hiss, where there is no weight and at the peak | white noise through `hp cutoff=4500`, `lp cutoff=11000`, `peak cutoff=6800 q=1.2 gain=6dB`; amp a 3 ms (the seeds do not arrive together), d 30 ms, s 0.2 (a few run on while the note is held), r 25 ms; `play: {event_stable: true, vel_sens: 0.6, gate: 1}`; fader -16 dB, pan 0.35, room -14 dB; a shake is a throw (touch 0.9) and a fall back (touch 0.8, 2 dB under); 5 dB harder where a shake is an eighth; throws on beats 2 3 4 written 4 2 4 dB up | as delivered the throw is -25.6 dBFS from 4.5 to 11 kHz (13.9 dB over the song on its bare beats, 5.8 at the peak) and the fall back 2.8 and 4.5 dB over; the master lifts it 8.6 to 10.9 dB over its bench; a touch of 0.55 beside 0.9 is 5 dB at this vel_sens | a hat in all but name: say so if it is used as one; do not put it on the off-beat where a metal is struck (0.9 dB over the song there) |

## Recipes

| recipe | how | who |
|---|---|---|
| A PART ON ANOTHER CLOCK: one function d(t) (beats ahead of the grid) places every event of a part (`t + d(t) = b`, solved by iteration) and tunes it (`12 log2(1 + d')` on a lane into `synth.pitch.mod`) | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t1.py`: `d`, `when`, `speed`, `spd_lane`, `deck_b` | chirper-zero, t1 v1 |
| A FORM FROM LEAST COMMON MULTIPLES: give each part a cycle, and place every event of the form where cycles meet (`s % c == 0` for two, for three, for three and the beat, for all and the bar); nothing is typed as a bar number, and changing one cycle rewrites the song | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t2.py`: `CYCLES`, `SUPER`, `PAGE`, `HALF`, `build`; `euclid` for the fills | chirper-one, t2 v1 |
| A ROOM OF TWO WALLS (a room is a rhythm): two buses, each with `returns` from the source (`-4dB`) and from the OTHER wall (`-3dB`) at its own distance (`time: "3/16"` and `"4/16"`), an eq for what the wall absorbs, then a door bus (pan, a `gain` on a lane). The echoes fall at a, b, a+b, 2a+b, a+2b, 2(a+b) ...; read in a bench to the sixteenth. A room whose length divides the bar stands; one that does not fills the bar. A source that repeats every bar sounds the same every bar in ANY room: to hear the room's cycle against the bar, strike on the room's own time | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t3.py`: `ROOMS`, `buses()`, `tock_notes()` | chirper-zero, t3 v1 |
| A CLICK IS A NOTE STOPPED TOO SOON: give a struck model its ring time per stroke (`decay: {value: t0, mod: [{src: expr.ring, amount: 1}]}`, the note carrying `expr: {ring: seconds}`), hold the amp envelope open, and write the form as each thing's ring against the bar. Read each thing on a bench first (`--bench`: one stroke at six rings; fall time, strongest partial, share of power on the note) | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t4.py`: `RING`, `SHAPE`, `ring_at`, `seconds`, `CAL`, `EASE`, `bench` | chirper-one, t4 v1 |
| A RISSET RHYTHM (an accelerando that never arrives): L layers, each twice as fast as the one below; stroke n of the finest falls at `T * log2(n / n0)` for n = n0 ... 2 n0 - 1 (n0 = C * 2^(L-1)); a stroke belongs to layer j when `n % 2^(L-1-j) == 0`; velocity = the sum of its layers' windows `sin^2(pi * (t / T + j) / L)` over L / 2, on a patch with `vel_sens 1`. The row repeats exactly (measured 1.000) while every layer doubles its speed. Choose C near `T / (2 ln 2)` (23 for 32 beats) and the layers start and end each row on the grid | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t5.py`: `risset`, `ris_notes` | chirper-zero, t5 v1 |
| A COMB WITH NO EFFECT, AND A PUMP WITH NO COMPRESSOR (phase as the only tool). Comb: two tracks, one patch with no noise and a fixed start phase (`saw phase=0`), the same notes a few ms apart: notches at odd multiples of 1 / (2 gap), +6 dB at multiples of 1 / gap (measured). Pump: pairs of `sine phase=0` whose frequencies differ by one cycle a beat (`ratio = h + (BPM / 60) / f0`), struck on the off-beat: they cancel on every beat | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t7.py`: `phi`, `when`, `b_notes`, `os-click`, `os-bass` | chirper-zero, t7 v1 |
| A PAIR OF COINS (an echo network that composes): two buses that `returns` a seed track, themselves and each other, each with `pitch` in the loop; an echo lands `p*a + q*b` steps late and `7*a - 5*b` semitones off. THE `pitch` EFFECT IS LATE: with its default grain about 14 ms a pass shifting up a fifth (11 to 18) and 18 ms down a fourth, and it peaks unevenly after several passes; with `grain 10ms` 3 to 5 ms a pass; with `grain 80ms` it wanders by tens of ms. Take the lateness off the return's time (written in ms) | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t6.py`: `PAIRS`, `LAG`, `G`, `lattice`; bench tools in the composer's notes | chirper-one, t6 v1 |
| A SOURCE WALKING ACROSS A ROOM: `returns` times are fixed, so a place is a pair of walls (a, L - a) with its own door; walk by opening the next place's door (one bar) as the last one's shuts (two). The room's length L, and anything timed to it, stays; the rhythm and the ear the first echo comes from change. 48 buses render a 5-minute song in 22 s | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t3.py`: `WALK`, `places()`, `door_lane` | chirper-zero, t3 v2 |
| THE SPRING RUNS DOWN: a `tempo` list whose last point has `ramp: true` (124 held to the last eight bars, then 50), strokes still written on the grid; the time of bar b is `60 / k * ln((124 + k * x) / 124)` for x beats in, k the fall in BPM a beat. Effects timed in ms (rooms, coins) do not follow it, so they are gone before it starts | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t8.py`: `WIND`, `SLOW`, `clock` | chirper-one, t8 v1 |
| A SHEPARD-RISSET (faster and higher for ever): give each layer of the Risset rhythm its own track on one pitched patch, the roots an octave apart, and one lane that lifts them all 12 semitones through the row and drops back; velocities are the layers' own windows (not summed). Layer j ends the row where layer j + 1 began it in speed and in pitch. Put the lowest root where another part masks it and the new bottom voice is not heard coming in | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t5.py`: `layer`, `shep_notes`, `rise_lane` | chirper-zero, t5 v2 |
| A RECORD SWITCHED OFF (the platter running down): give the deck's hand a last shape, `d(t) = -x^2 / 2w` for x beats after the motor goes off and w beats of run-down (then `-w/2 - (x - w)`): the speed `1 + d'` falls in a straight line to nothing, the strokes come at `x = w - sqrt(w^2 - 2w * beats)` and only w / 2 beats of record are ever played; the same speed lane sinks every pitch (rest it five octaves down, a stopped record has none). Leave something that never moved (the grid click) to count on alone | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t1.py`: `d` (`stop`), `stop_at`, `when` | chirper-zero, t1 v3 |
| A LOOP ROLL THAT BECOMES A NOTE (Zeno's bar): repeat the first instant of a drum, each note as long as the loop, on a patch that lets go in 1 ms; give every loop the same number of turns (16) and make each loop half the last: the turns take 2 beats, 1, a half ... and fill the bar only in the limit. Under about 30 turns a second it is a roll; above it a note, an octave up at each halving. The note is the TEMPO's (BPM / 60 x 2^k: at 124 a C 19 cents sharp), not the song's. Write `at` to six decimals of a beat (four smears the top loops) and stop near 2 kHz (a loop of 23 samples) | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t1.py`: `roll_stages`, `roll_notes` | chirper-zero, t1 v5 |
| A RECORD SLOWED WITHOUT ITS PITCH (the stretch, by grains; Zeno's record): cut the part into grains (here a sixteenth); play each grain whole, at full speed, and start the next one only 1/N of a grain further into the part. Every stroke is then heard N times, (1 - 1/N) of a grain apart, the last copy where a record N times slower would have it; a stroke is cut off at its grain's end. N = 2, 4, 8, 16 over equal times gets through 1, 1/2, 1/4, 1/8 of the distance: it never arrives, and its limit is a loop of one grain. No engine field: it is all notes, so the pitch cannot move. TAKE THE OTHER DECK'S DRUM OUT from under it, or it is not found (and here the form then stood on one cut) | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t1.py`: `grains`, `stretched`, `STRETCH_BY`, `GRAIN`, `STRETCH_OVER` | chirper-zero, t1 v6 |
| A RECORD SPUN BACK (the rewind): for one bar play what the record holds BACKWARDS: the record's place is p0 - s0·tau·(1 - e^(-x/tau)), so a stroke at record beat b sounds at x = -tau·ln(1 - (p0 - b)/(s0·tau)); choose s0 so that it stops on a bar line (s0 = back/(tau·(1 - e^(-T/tau)))); the pitch lane is 12·log2 of the speed (a straight line down in semitones), with a point 0.01 beat before each end so the lane jumps on the beat; lengths divided by the speed. Then start whatever comes next (here the stretch) from where the record stopped. Keep the bars it goes back over the same parity as what the next section needs | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t1.py`: `rewind`, `rewind_speed`, `rewound`, `REWIND_*` | chirper-zero, t1 v7 |
| THE OTHER SPEED (a polyrhythm from a deck's switch): for a window of 3n bars play the record's bars 4/3 as fast: a stroke at record beat b (from the window's first bar) sounds at b·3/4, its length ·3/4, and the pitch lane is 12·log2(4/3) = +4.98 semitones (a perfect fourth: tune the record so a fourth up is still in the key). The record's bar is 3 grid beats: its bar lines walk 1, 4, 3, 2 and meet the grid every third bar. Jump the lane 0.01 beat before each end. Any m/n does it: 3/2 is a fifth and three bars in two | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t1.py`: `fast_window`, `forced`, `sped`, `FAST_BY`, `FAST_BARS` | chirper-zero, t1 v8 |
| AN ECHO PRESSED INTO THE RECORD (an echo that follows the hand): a bus delay is on the grid's clock and repeats the pitch that went in. To put the echo on a record whose speed moves, write each repeat as NOTES at the record's own beats (b + k·time, through the same time map as the part) on tracks carrying the same pitch lane: the repeats crowd and string out with the speed and each is pitched by the speed when it sounds. Fit the levels to the bus on a bench of ONE chord first (here: gain = the part's gain + the send + 20·log10(feedback)·(k - 1) + 20·log10(0.7), a low-pass that falls with k, hp as the bus's, and a send of -7.36 dB to a wet-only hall for the bus's mix 0.3). A SEND IS TAKEN AFTER THE TRACK'S GAIN AND PAN (benched: 20 dB less gain is 20 dB less echo) | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t1.py`: `echo_span`, `echo_notes`, `ECHO_*` | chirper-zero, t1 v9 |
| THE NEEDLE STICKS (a polymeter from one turn of a platter): one turn at 33 is 1.81 s: L sixteenths of the record at the song's tempo (15 at 124). For N turns the record's part at sixteenth i of the window plays what it holds at sixteenth i mod L of the window's first bar, each stroke placed by the deck's own clock (`when(p0 + i/4)`), so a hand on the platter still bends it; a pop at every jump; the notes the needle never reads are left out; after N turns a hand puts the needle on. The part's bar then comes a sixteenth earlier each turn: L against 16. A pattern with one long gap (0 3 6 9 12) goes even; a four-on-the-floor goes round the grid's | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t1.py`: `stick`, `stuck`, `pops`, `STICK_*`; patch `gt-pop` | chirper-zero, t1 v10 |
| A SCRATCH WITHOUT A SAMPLE (the hand on the record; a record's pitch AND loudness are its speed): hold the sound that is on the record (gate 1, amp sustained) and write three lanes from the hand's crank `theta = 2 pi (t - t0)/T + phi`: speed `v = V sin(theta)`, place `p = (V T / 2 pi)(cos(phi) - cos(theta))` seconds past the sound's start. Pitch lane `12 log2 |v|` on the synth's `pitch`; a `gain` fx lane `20 log10(|v| x amp(p))`, -80 where `p < 0`; a cutoff lane from the sound's filter envelope at `p`. With `phi = pi/4`, `V = sqrt 2` the start goes under the needle ON the beat at the record's own speed and the rub sounds for 3/4 of its time. Points every 1/64 beat, `curve: 0`, and an extra point 0.002 beats before each start and after each cut (the cut read 40 dB down in 3 ms). DRY: a send would carry the held chord. Take the deck's own parts out of the bar | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t1.py`: `scratch_spans`, `rubs`, `hand_at`, `hand_lanes`, `SCR_*`; patch `gt-rub` | chirper-zero, t1 v11 |
| ONE RECORD AT FIVE SPEEDS (a canon by proportion, written by the record's own law): deck k plays the same strokes at 1/2^k of the speed: each stroke sounds when the fast deck's record is 2^k times as far in (`when(start + x * 2^k)`), k octaves down, and its PATCH has every time multiplied by 2^k (ring, rise, fall, release) and the same pitch lane. All start together; choose the span so the slowest gets through one bar. At one fader every deck gives the same power (half the strokes, twice as long each). Read each deck ALONE (a pair to a render, one a channel), then in the master read every stroke's line against the old version's at that note: the low decks land on the bass's notes and need lifting; say so | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t1.py`: `speeds_span`, `speeds_notes`, `SPEEDS_*`; patches `gt-tock-2` … `-16` | chirper-zero, t1 v12 |
| WHICH ROWS HAVE NOTHING THERE (mending a band that is SHORT): the scorer's band is a mean of dB over time, so an empty stretch costs far more than a quiet one. Read the master row by row (8 bars) in the short bands (`lowm.py`): here two rows of eighteen were 30 dB down under 160 Hz and were most of the deficit. Put a part THERE that belongs there (here the other record's bassline, where deck A's bass is out), on the notes of the short bands (D2 is 73 Hz, C3 131 Hz), and keep it under the music: read the section's level before and after, and what is left after fitting the old version (a new low part reads far louder than its fader: -10 dB was louder than the rest of the section; -15 dB is 2.5 dB under it). Expect the loudness stage to take 0.1 to 0.5 dB off the rest of the song, and the section cuts round it to weaken | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t1.py`: `BASSB_*`; patch `gt-bass-b` | chirper-zero, t1 v13 |
| THE CUE (a part heard in one ear, out of step, coming into step, before it comes in for everyone): write the part's own strokes for the bars before its entry on a second track, hard to one side and dry, through a small-speaker band; place each stroke late by `D0 * cos(pi u / H) * 2 ** (-u / H)` beats (u beats after the hand takes it): the hand over-corrects, half as far every H beats. D0 = half a sixteenth is the furthest a sixteenth-note part can be from the grid; H = 2 beats finds it in two bars (within 4 ms). Hold it a few bars, then let the real part in where it always came in. Read it alone by RISE (the error at each half bar) and in the master by new less old over 1.5 kHz against the click, the room's version of the part, and what the music leaves between its strokes (set the level there: 11 dB over the gap, 4 under the room's part) | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t1.py`: `cue_err`, `cue_notes`, `CUE_*`; `~/.local/state/chirper-zero/c11/t1-v14/b/cuem.py`, `cuemm.py` | chirper-zero, t1 v14 |
| WHERE THE SCORER'S RHYTHM TERM CAN BE MOVED (find the place before the idea): add trial strokes to the song's own onset curve and re-read the off-grid share (`b/r1.py WAV BPM`; put them 3 frames early, as real onsets show: without that the reading comes out backwards). In Give or Take: strokes on the odd sixteenths of 16 busy bars moved it 0.036 to 0.05; of the 16 opening bars (drum and click only) to 0.08-0.14. Real: 4 held bars and 2 searching, at onset height 291 against the click's 1100: 0.036 to 0.072 (rhythm 66.8 to 83.2). A beat-long stream on one odd sixteenth, triplets and fives do less than every odd sixteenth | `~/.local/state/chirper-zero/c11/t1-v14/b/r1.py` | chirper-zero, t1 v14 |
| A RECORD HEARD A TURN BEFORE IT PLAYS (pre-echo; a foreshadowing that is true to vinyl): copy the loudest part's strokes to a new track, move each one turn of the platter EARLIER (33 turns a minute = 1.8 s; choose the tempo's nearest whole number of sixteenths: 15 at 124), keep 250 Hz to 3 kHz, 14 dB under the source's fader, dry, same place. Drop the copies that would fall before the song starts. In steady playing a copy 15 sixteenths early is a sixteenth LATE in the bar before and cannot be told from an echo; it shows itself wherever the part STARTS after a rest (the copies sound alone, a bar early) and STOPS (the copies stop a bar before it does). `pre_bars()` lists both kinds of bar. An ODD number of sixteenths to the turn puts every copy on an odd sixteenth: this took Give or Take's off-grid share 0.072 to 0.177 and its rhythm term 83 to 100, and the copies' low edge (a drum through a 250 Hz high-pass still has 114 to 250 Hz) mended those bands by COVERAGE (-7.0 to -5.3 at 133 Hz). Read it with new less old fitted, row by row, on sixteenth 1 and sixteenth 3 | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t1.py`: `pre_notes`, `pre_bars`, `PRE_*`; `~/.local/state/chirper-zero/c11/t1-v15/b/prem.py` | chirper-zero, t1 v15 |
| A CLOCK THAT DOES NOT CARE (a real-time pulse under a tempo: one tick at every whole SECOND of the song): write the ticks at beats = seconds x BPM / 60. At 124 fifteen seconds are thirty-one beats, so the tick slides a fifteenth of a beat against every second beat and is exactly on a beat once in fifteen seconds; choose the passage so that one of those meetings falls where the drum is (here 2:00.0 = bar 63, beat 1). Put it where nothing else keeps time (a break, a passage with a wandering deck). A tick is three sounds 14, 8 and 0 ms before the second (unlock, impulse, drop: velocities 0.35, 0.5, 1); tick and tock are the same grain a minor third apart. EXPECT: the master ducks single ticks by up to 16 dB with whatever else is sounding (median 14.6 dB under the music's peaks at -20 dB, from 6 to 22), and where a tick meets the drum it is under it, not beside it. The scorer does not count them (1 more onset in 31 ticks) | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t1.py`: `clock_span`, `clock_seconds`, `clock_notes`, `CLOCK_*`; `~/.local/state/chirper-zero/c11/t1-v16/b/clockm.py` | chirper-zero, t1 v16 |
| A PART THAT WAS RUNNING ALL ALONG (bring a part back where the song is bare, on the phase it would have had): keep its clock absolute (here: every whole second of the song; the pallet by the parity of the second), give the return ITS OWN TRACKS AND FADER, and set that fader in the whole song's master: the same tick that reads -15.9 dBFS at -20 dB under a busy passage reads 7.8 dB OVER its fader in a bare ending (-40 dB gave -32.2 dBFS, -50 gave -42.2: exactly 10 apart, so one bench render fixes the fader for any level wanted). FOR A SEAM: agree the level with the owner of the next song's first bars in THEIR band (chirper-one: Least Common's page one stands near -55 dBFS from 2 to 5.5 kHz, so -36 is 19 over it), and give them the beats in their tempo (seconds x their BPM / 60). CHECK WHAT THE ARITHMETIC DOES AT THE END: at 124 a whole second is on a sixteenth only every 15 s, and one second either side of the half-way point (277.5 s) the tick is 16 ms from a click stroke: the last tick here is ducked 7.7 dB by the click before it | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t1.py`: `clock_end_seconds`, `clock_end_notes`, `CLOCK_END_*`; `~/.local/state/chirper-zero/c11/t1-v17/b/endm.py` | chirper-zero, t1 v17 |
| A PART THAT TELLS THE SONG'S OWN TIME (a clock that strikes): put strokes at whole SECONDS of the song (`at = seconds * BPM / 60` beats), k strokes at the k-th minute, a second apart. Find where the tempo and the clock agree: at 124 a minute is 124 beats, 31 bars, so every minute begins on a bar line; a second is 2 beats and a fifteenth, so each next stroke is 1/31 s (32.3 ms) further behind its beat (chirper-one's arithmetic, from Wind Down). A listener can check it against the player's clock. WHAT IT COST TO LEARN: (1) THE MASTER DELIVERS THE SAME STROKE 10 dB APART IN DIFFERENT PLACES: written at one level, the strokes in a thin passage (the stretch, 3:00) came out at -4 dBFS and those in full passages at -14. Read every stroke in the whole song's master and trim by place (here per minute, in the velocity: vel_sens 0.5 is linear). (2) a ringing sound is delivered where a tick is not (chirper-one's finding, confirmed): the bell's ring stands 10 to 22 dB over the music in its band though its stroke falls with the drum | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t1.py`: `chime_strikes`, `chime_notes`, `chime_hz`, `CHIME_*`; `~/.local/state/chirper-zero/c11/t1-v18/b/chimemm.py` (every stroke in the master) | chirper-zero, t1 v18 |
| A COUNT-IN IN ANOTHER UNIT (the pips): to make a moment of the song's own time heard COMING, put a short tone on each of the n seconds before it (`at = seconds * BPM / 60` beats) and a long one that begins on it. Where the moment is on a bar line (at 124, every whole minute) and a second is not a whole number of beats, the tones are a fixed step nearer the beat each time (here 1/31 s: 161, 129, 97, 65, 32, 0 ms early): a flam that closes. WHAT IT COST TO LEARN: (1) THE MASTER DELIVERS EQUAL TONES 7 dB APART INSIDE ONE MINUTE: the ones nearest a beat come out smallest, because it ducks with the drum. A signal whose whole character is that its pips are equal needs a trim for every pip, read from one whole-song render (target less measured, in the velocity; put the fader up by the largest lift so no velocity goes over 0.9): after it all 24 are within 0.4 dB on the seed they were set on and 1.0 dB on the other. (2) The trim does not un-duck: a pip the drum lands in is still cut to half height after the drum (35 to 66 ms of 100), and a long tone that begins on a downbeat is under it for its first 67 to 114 ms. Say so; do not chase it. (3) A plain 1 kHz tone 100 ms long needs no more than the music has in its third of an octave to be heard as itself: at -27 dBFS it stands 3 to 14 dB over the music between 800 and 1250 Hz in most places. | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t1.py`: `pips_seconds`, `pips_notes`, `pips_note_hz`, `PIPS_*`; `~/.local/state/chirper-zero/c11/t1-v19/b/pipsmm.py` (every pip in the master; with `-` for the new file, the music at each pip's place) | chirper-zero, t1 v19 |
| A SCRATCH ACROSS THE RECORD (the record's own clock): if a deck's parts are written as (sixteenth of the RECORD, note) and turned into grid time by one function (the hand, the stretch, the spin back, the stuck groove, the other speed, the stop), a scratch is one more part: a tick on every N-th sixteenth of the record (N = the sixteenths in one turn: 15 at 124), and it comes out early, late, repeated, backwards, closer and at last not at all with no new arithmetic. WHAT IT COST TO LEARN: (1) PUT IT BETWEEN THE RECORD'S OWN STROKES. On a sixteenth every tick fell with deck B's click: 2 to 5 dB UNDER the music in its own 8 ms at -12 dB, an accent on the click; half a sixteenth on, the same tick at -16 dB is 4 to 15 dB OVER what is in its gap. (2) read what the song's own clicks are in the master first (here -14 to -26 dBFS in 8 ms on the sixteenths, -22 to -52 between) and set the tick under the first and over the second. (3) this master delivers the same tick 12 dB apart by place (the ones just after a bass-drum stroke are the small ones): left as it is, a scratch is not even. (4) WHAT A PART DOES NOT DO CAN BE THE FINDING: a stuck needle changes everything on the record but the scratch, which was once a turn already. (5) choose the tick's two tones by `--bands` (here 1 kHz and 3.2 kHz had room, 2.2 kHz is at the edge): the spectrum term then reads the choice, +0.03 here; say so. (6) A PATCH SCRIPT WHOSE ASSERT FAILS WRITES NOTHING: chain the generator runs and the renders behind it with `&&`, every one of them, or renders start on the old generator. | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t1.py`: `scar_phase`, `scar_steps`, `scar_notes`, `SCAR_*`; `~/.local/state/chirper-zero/c11/t1-v20/b/scarmm.py` (imports the generator and reads every tick in the master, section by section), `b/rowsk.py`, `b/scar.yaml` (the tick alone) | chirper-zero, t1 v20 |
| A TUNE ON A CLOCK'S TIME, NOT THE SONG'S (the quarters of a minute chimed in seconds over a song in beats): write the tune in SECONDS and turn it into beats (`sec * BPM / 60`): at 124 BPM half a second is a beat and a thirtieth, so each note is a thirtieth of a beat later against the grid than the last and a twelve-note chime ends half a beat behind where it began (its last note is 7.5 s, 15.5 beats, after its first); a quarter of a minute is 7.75 bars, so each chime begins a beat earlier in the bar. The Westminster quarters are five changes on four bells (by degree of the scale 3 2 1 5, 1 3 2 5, 1 2 3 1, 3 1 2 5, 5 2 3 1; the fourth note three times as long), taken in order: one at the first quarter, two at the half, three at the third. WHAT IT COST TO LEARN: (1) A PITCHED PART IS CHARGED AT THE OTHER END OF THE SPECTRUM: at -25 dB the bells raise the bands from 641 to 3078 Hz by 0.3 to 1.2 dB, every other band falls 0.16 dB against the album's shape and the band already on its low edge (71 Hz, -6.0) goes to -6.2: -0.013 at -28 dB, -0.017 at -25, -0.023 at -22 (the bands move nearly as much at -25 as at -22: it is how long the bells sound, not how loud). I did not move the bells for it, and reckon any bells would cost the same (not tried). (2) THIS MASTER DELIVERS A BELL AS WRITTEN (6 dB of fader was 6 dB of bell; unlike a click, which it holds at one level): set the level so that the QUIETEST note of the tune stands over the music in a band 12% wide round its own tone: a tune needs every note. (3) twelve of thirteen chimes came out within 3 dB of one another; the one in the section with no kick was 3 dB up: trim by the chime, in the velocity. (4) the bell is the patch the minutes are struck on: no new patch; the chimes say which clock it is. | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t1.py`: `quart_chimes`, `quart_notes`, `QUART_*`; `~/.local/state/chirper-zero/c11/t1-v21/b/quartmm.py` (imports the generator; chime by chime, every note in its own band against the old music there, and found by its rise) | chirper-zero, t1 v21 |
| A CANON BY ACCELERATION (Nancarrow's Canon X, as a record does it: two copies of one part, one winding up while the other runs down, crossing once): give each copy a speed that is a straight line in PITCH, `2 ** (±R (t - tc) / H)` (R octaves each way, H beats each side of the crossing tc); the record's beat under its needle is the speed added up, `tc ± k (speed - 1)` with `k = H / (R ln 2)`, set so that BOTH are at the record's beat tc at tc; a stroke at record beat b sounds at `tc ± (H / R) log2(1 ± (b - tc) / k)`. Write each copy's strokes at those times on the part's own patch, with the pitch lane the patch already follows (semitones: `±12 R (t - tc) / H`, four points a beat, `curve: 0`) and a pan lane the same shape (`pan: {value: 0, mod: [{src: auto.walk, amount: 1}]}`): the two cross in tempo, in pitch and in place at one stroke, which is the only one they play together (here on a bar line, in key, on the grid). R = 1 puts both ends an octave out, so the ends are in key too. The same beat of the record is `R ln 2 * x^2 / H` beats apart on the two copies x beats from the crossing (46 ms at one beat, 204 at two, for H = 8 at 124): a flam, then a gallop, then two lines. WHAT IT COST TO LEARN: (1) LOOK FOR WHAT ELSE IS PITCHED IN THE ROW BEFORE CHOOSING ITS BARS: the clock's chimes come every 7.75 bars and their bells are where the slow copy's notes are: four clear bars, not eight. (2) the master delivered these ticks as written (6 dB of fader, 6 dB of each stroke); read every stroke in a band round ITS OWN moving note against the old music there. (3) the two strokes either side of the crossing do not rise: the other copy's stroke on the same note is 24 to 42 ms before them. (4) the ring does not stretch with the speed unless the patch's times ride a lane too: said, not mended. | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t1.py`: `canon_span`, `canon_speed`, `canon_place`, `canon_when`, `canon_notes`, `canon_lanes`, `CANON_*`; `~/.local/state/chirper-zero/c11/t1-v22/b/canmm.py` (imports the generator: every stroke in the master, in its own moving band: over the music, rise, side), `b/canal.py` and `b/canon.yaml` (alone: time, pitch, pan law) | chirper-zero, t1 v22 |
| WOW (a record with its hole off centre: Give or Take v23): the speed under the needle goes `off / radius` up and down once a turn (1 mm at 80 mm: 1.25%, 21.5 cents each way). Add `12 log2(1 + e cos(2 pi turns(t)))` semitones to the one pitch lane every part of the record follows; count `turns` in the RECORD's sixteenths (15 to a turn here), so that at another speed the wow goes faster with it (33 to 45: four turns for three), and from a mark the song already has (the scratch's tick: the crest is on the tick). STROKES DO NOT SHOW IT: hold a chord of plain tones on the record (sines: nothing in it may move but the record: a unison or a chorus would hide 20 cents). WHAT IT COST TO LEARN: (1) TO READ A HELD PART AMONG PARTS THAT CHANGED TOO, RENDER THE SAME SONG WITHOUT IT (a switch for the part alone) AND FIT THAT OUT: against the old version the row is 14 dB changed by the strokes' own wow. (2) read the pitch on a note every chord has (D4), 120 ms every 30 ms, strongest frequency within 4% of where it is written, and fit a cosine and sine of the written turn: amplitude, phase, r. (3) a lane with a point a sixteenth reads 22.1 cents at 15 points a turn and 20.0 at 11. (4) the master pumps a held chord 4.9 dB a beat. (5) A HELD CHORD COSTS: spectrum (a part between 180 and 400 Hz reads 0.1 dB off the 71 Hz band, at -28, -31 and -34 dB alike: 99.972, 99.974, 99.975 against 99.981) and likeness to the album's other held-tone song (+0.006): the wow on the strokes alone costs nothing and is not heard. (6) FIND WHERE THE DECK IS PLAIN FIRST: every row but four bars had a hand, a stuck needle, a canon or the other speed on it. | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t1.py`: `wow_row`, `wow_rec`, `wow_turns`, `wow_st`, `wow_notes`, `WOW_*`, and the one changed line in `spd_lane`; `~/.local/state/chirper-zero/c11/t1-v23/b/wowmm.py` (imports the generator: the organ against the reference: level, pump, and the wow read from D4), `b/wow.yaml` (alone) | chirper-zero, t1 v23 |
| A RECORD STARTED FROM STANDSTILL (the stop's mirror; Give or Take v24): if the platter's speed rises in a straight line to full over `w` beats, the record has turned `t^2 / (2 w)` beats after t: its k-th sixteenth comes by at `t = sqrt(2 w k / 4)` (w = 8: 2 times the root of k), at speed `t / w`. Write each as one note of CHIP NOISE with `clock: 0` (a shift register begun from the same state at every note, clocked from the note's pitch): pitch `12 log2(speed)` on a lane held flat over the note, length `L / speed`, velocity `accent x speed`: then every click is THE SAME STEPS of the register, slower: what a groove is. Put what does NOT slow down (the headphone, the room) after it as fixed filters. Land the last one on a stroke that is already there (here the cue's first). WHAT IT COST TO LEARN: (1) the register's click is 16.8 dB over a dying-noise click at one fader: READ ONE CLICK OF EACH ALONE and trim, then check the hand-over in the master (the last four against the first four that follow: within 3.3 dB). (2) THE CLICKS WHOSE NUMBER IS A SQUARE FALL A WHOLE NUMBER OF BEATS AFTER THE START: if the start is on (or just after) a bass drum, so are they, and this master ducks them 8 to 12 dB. (3) a slowed click's ENERGY falls 3 dB a halving of speed (its amplitude 6, its length doubles): read levels by energy and say which. (4) the lane is taken at the note's start: the noise's turns over the speed are the same to 4%. (5) the cost is 0.002 (spectrum). NOT DONE: the record's other parts (the tock) coming up with it; a real direct-drive deck is up in 0.7 s, this one takes the two bars its stop takes. | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t1.py`: `up_span`, `up_ticks`, `UP_*`; `~/.local/state/chirper-zero/c11/t1-v24/b/upmm.py` (imports the generator: every click in the master, its ear and band: level, against the music in its time, against the last click beside the written, and the hand-over to the cue), `b/upal.py` and `b/up.yaml` (alone: start, length x speed, level with and without the headphone, turns over speed, one click against the cue's) | chirper-zero, t1 v24 |
| TWO COPIES OF ONE TONE BEAT, AND THE BEATS COUNT THE DRIFT (Give or Take v25): put the same held plain tone on both decks: one track with a flat pitch lane, one with the moving deck's speed lane (semitones); ONE NOTE EACH for the whole passage, started together where the decks are in step, both in the middle. The moving copy gets ahead by f times the drift in waves, so the sum is 2 cos(pi f drift): a silence wherever the decks are an odd number of half waves apart. CHOOSE THE TONE FROM THE TEMPO: 64 waves to the beat (BPM x 64 / 60 Hz: 132.27 Hz at 124) makes a throb a 64th of a beat and a quarter-beat nudge 16 throbs exactly. WHAT IT COST TO LEARN: (1) THE ENGINE KEEPS PHASE THROUGH A PITCH LANE: 16 waves out and 16 back, twice, on a lane of one point a sixteenth (3 decimals of a semitone): all 64 silences within 6 ms of where the arithmetic puts them (median 1.8 ms) alone, 3 ms in the master. (2) a new note starts its wave again: never re-strike either tone inside the passage. (3) both tones in ONE place (pan 0): an ear each would make the beat in the head (Hearing Things' binaural beats), not in the air. (4) IN THIS MASTER a held tone at 132 Hz is delivered about 6 dB for 6 of fader; -31 dB each stands the swells about 5 dB over the rest of the music in its band. (5) a low held tone pays in a song short at 60 to 250 Hz. | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t1.py`: `beat_row`, `beat_hz`, `beat_off`, `beat_apart`, `beat_nulls`, `BEAT_*` (and `d`, `speed`, `spd_lane`); `~/.local/state/chirper-zero/c11/t1-v25/b/beatmm.py` (imports the generator: the old music fitted out; the envelope in the tone's band; every written silence: how far the lowest point is from it and how deep; the swells against the old music) and `b/beat.yaml` (the two tones alone) | chirper-zero, t1 v25 |
| A LEAP SECOND (Give or Take v26): when every event of a part is written from one clock (seconds of the clock turned into beats), a second can be given or taken with ONE function between the clock's second and the song's (`leap_real`): add 1 from the given minute to the taken one, nought elsewhere; the given minute gets a sixth short pip on the second its long pip used to begin on and its long pip a second later; the taken minute loses its last short pip. Everything written through the function (the strokes, the quarters) moves with it and nothing else does; the part's last events are where they were, so a seam that depends on them is not touched. WHAT IT TAUGHT: (1) LEVELS SET PIP BY PIP IN THE MASTER BELONG TO THE SECOND OF THE SONG, NOT TO THE PIP: a pip keeps the trim of the instant it stands on (the nine short pips and the long one that stayed put arrived as in v25 to 0.1 dB on both seeds; the pip on 3:00, short where it was long, 1.5 and 1.9 dB under the long one that stood there); only the one new instant, the long pip on 3:01, needed a reading, and 2 dB. (2) THE SCORER DID NOT SEE IT: one pip more, one fewer and sixteen bell notes moved by a second: no term moved on either seed. (3) the quarters moved by a second arrived 0.6 to 1.9 dB louder (they fall elsewhere in the bar): still inside the 3 dB the thirteen chimes span. | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t1.py`: `leap_real`, `pips_seconds`, `pips_trim`, `chime_strikes`, `quart_chimes`, `LEAP_*`; `~/.local/state/chirper-zero/c11/t1-v26/b/leapmm.py` (imports the generator twice, with the leap and without: every pip, stroke and quarter read as a tone at its own frequency over its own time, new render against old) | chirper-zero, t1 v26 |
| SWING AS A RATIO, WALKED LIKE INTERVALS (Give or Take v27, the grid is found): share each pair of sixteenths long to short as small whole numbers and walk them: 3:1, 2:1, 3:2, 1:1 (the off sixteenth at 3/4, 2/3, 3/5, 1/2 of its eighth: late by 1/8, 1/12, 1/20 of a beat and nothing: at 124 that is 60.5, 40.3, 24.2 ms), two bars a step; move ONLY the off sixteenths of one part, after the song is built. WHAT IT TAUGHT: (1) THE MASTER DELIVERS TIMING TO THE RATIO: from the tops of the hat's envelope (6 to 12.5 kHz) the pairs read 3.02 / 3.03, 2.00 / 2.01, 1.50 / 1.50, 1.00 / 1.00 (seeds 0 / 11), each off stroke 2.4 to 2.7 ms after its written instant, swung or not (the hat's own rise). (2) the off strokes keep their level when moved: tops -17.4 to -18.9 dBFS (they were -17.1 to -19.0), 14 dB or more over the 15 ms before them. (3) THE SCORER READS RHYTHM IN ITS LIKENESS: 48 hat strokes moved by 24 to 60 ms raised the likeness to Out of Step (a song of clicks out of step) from 0.821 to 0.831 and 0.837 to 0.840, with no note added: watch the edge (0.85) when only TIMING changes. (4) a part that comes in swung makes a weaker boundary than one that comes in straight: the three coarsest cuts no longer cut at the hat's entry (16 s); every term stayed 100. (5) the off-grid share fell (0.18 to 0.15): the scorer's grid is eighths, and the hat's off sixteenths, moved off the sixteenth, repeat less. | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t1.py`: `SWING`, `SWING_IN`, `SWING_PART`, `SWING_WALK`, `SWING_BARS`, `swing_bar`, `swing_off`, the block at the end of `song()`; `~/.local/state/chirper-zero/c11/t1-v27/b/swingmm.py` (reads a track's notes from two yamls and its strokes' tops in two renders: where each off stroke is, the pair's long to short as measured, its level and its rise) | chirper-zero, t1 v27 |
| A DRIFT MADE INTO A TUNE (the strobe; Give or Take v28): to let an ear follow how far one part is from another, flash at fixed instants of the ruler (here every beat of the grid) and play the scale step round(DOTS x d), d being the part's lead at the flash; choose DOTS so that the furthest reach is a few steps (here 50 dots a beat: the hand's 0.08 of a beat is 4 steps) and choose the flash instants so that the steps make a chord (on the beats a breath of two bars is 0 3 4 3, 0 -3 -4 -3; on the off-beats it was 2 4 4 2). WHAT IT TAUGHT: (1) THE DRIFT CAN BE READ FROM THE RENDER, AND THE TUNE CHECKED AGAINST IT: the right channel less the left at 7 to 14 kHz leaves deck B's hat alone (the drum and the click are in the middle); its stroke at each flash, taken where the highest peak within 70 ms begins, stood 38.5 ms ahead at +4 steps, 27.3 at +3, 9.8 at +1, 0.0 at 0, 20.7 behind at -2, 28.6 at -3, 38.8 at -4: 9.43 ms a step for a written 9.68, likeness 0.998 over the 32 flashes (taking the FIRST rise in the window instead found another part's stroke at four flashes). (2) A NOTE ON THE BASS DRUM IS LIFTED 10.8 dB BY THIS MASTER, NOT THE 18.5 OF A PART BETWEEN THE DRUMS: the first fader, reckoned with 18.5, came out 6 dB under what was meant. (3) the scorer: 0.001 for the first fader, 0.003 / 0.004 for the second: spectrum (the 71 Hz band is the only charged one and nothing here is in it); likeness to Out of Step 0.831 to 0.836 / 0.840 to 0.838. | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t1.py`: `STROBE`, `STROBE_*`, `strobe_row`, `strobe_pitch`, `strobe_notes`; `~/.local/state/chirper-zero/c11/t1-v28/b/strobemm.py` (imports the generator; each note's body level and pitch in the new less the old), `b/hatmm.py` (the hat's stroke against the grid at each flash, from the render), `b/sbal.yaml` | chirper-zero, t1 v28 |
| A TUNE GIVEN AND TAKEN A NOTE AT A TIME (Rzewski's process; Give or Take v29): passes of 1, 2, ... n notes from the front, then n-1, ... 1 notes to the end: n(n+1)/2 + n(n-1)/2 = n squared notes: choose n so that n squared steps are a whole number of bars (8 notes in eighths: 8 bars). The first note falls on the triangular numbers (later and later), the last on their mirror (sooner and sooner): the form ends by thinning to one note, so nothing stops dead. WHAT IT TAUGHT: (1) THE MASTER'S LIFT BELONGS TO THE ROW, NOT ONLY TO THE PLACE IN THE BEAT: where both decks play (two bass drums, sub, stab) a note a sixteenth after a stroke was lifted 10.6 dB, no more than a note ON the drum in a thinner row (a bare break gives 17 to 18.5): take the first fader from a reading in that row. (2) IN A FULL ROW THE TWO OFF-SIXTEENTHS ARE DELIVERED ALIKE (-28.6 and -28.3 dBFS; nine notes in ten within 1.6 dB): no trims by place were needed, as they were for ticks that fell with strokes. (3) BUT THEY ARE NOT MASKED ALIKE: a sixteenth after the beat the old music at 150 to 3500 Hz is 2 dB over the note, a sixteenth after the off-beat 8 dB over it (the sub and the stab ring there): level is not audibility: print the old music's tops by sixteenth in the part's band first, and say both. (4) a soft tune over a whole row, aligned to the row, moved no cut's bounds at the finest and the coarsest (one middle cut on one seed found a section more) and cost 0.001; likeness to Out of Step rose 0.002 (0.836 / 0.839: 0.011 left). (5) a reader that imports a generator must not assume its helpers: this one has no `hz`. | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t1.py`: `GIVE`, `GIVE_*`, `give_row`, `give_passes`, `give_notes`; `~/.local/state/chirper-zero/c11/t1-v29/b/givemm.py` (imports the generator; every note's level and pitch in its first 100 ms, by place, by pass and by note, and against the old music, in a bench or in the new less the old), `b/giveal.yaml` | chirper-zero, t1 v29 |
| A TONE WITH AN EXACT HALF-LIFE (Give or Take v30): a held plain tone whose whole shape is its track's lane, in dB: `fx: [{"type": "gain", "gain": {"value": "0dB", "mod": [{"src": "auto.life", "amount": 1}]}}]` and a lane with `curve 0` from 0 down in a straight line: dB by time in a straight line IS a half-life (6.0206 dB a half-life). Alone it reads 5.91 dB a bar for 6.02 written (the reader's first beat holds the 4 ms it takes to come). WHAT IT TAUGHT: (1) THE MASTER'S LIFT BELONGS TO THE ROW (again): in this song's full rows a held low tone 60 ms after the drum was lifted 10.4 to 10.6 dB, as v29's tune was, not the 17 a held tone had elsewhere: I guessed 17, read 10.6 and raised the fader 10 dB before any score: READ THE SONG'S OWN LAST ROUND FOR THE LIFT BEFORE THE FIRST FADER. (2) THE MASTER SLOWS A FALL A LITTLE: 5.73 for 6.02 dB a bar, 11.65 for 12.04, 17.52 for 18.06 (it turns up what is quiet). (3) A TONE 12 dB UNDER THE MUSIC IN ITS OWN BAND CANNOT BE READ in the new less the old (the fourth tone: 20.8 for 24.08): say so rather than quote it as its fall. (4) ONE HELD LOW NOTE WHERE THE SONG'S ONE SHORT BAND WAS EMPTY PAID THE WHOLE SPECTRUM TERM: 15.5 s of D2 at -25.6 dBFS falling took 71 Hz from -6.3 to -5.9 and the song to 100.0 on both seeds (chirper-one's finding, used: the scorer averages a band in dB over frames). That is the scorer's reading and says nothing of what is heard. (5) LIKENESS MOVED WITHOUT A RHYTHM: to Out of Step 0.836 to 0.843 on seed 0 and 0.838 to 0.835 on seed 11: read both seeds; the edge is 0.85. | RISSET'S RHYTHM (Give or Take v35): L beats at once, the kth with N 2^k strokes in a turn of T beats, every one's speed r 2^(k + t/T) with r T = N ln 2: so the kth beat's jth stroke falls T log2(1 + j / (N 2^k)) beats into the turn, a stroke of a slower beat is always a stroke of every faster one, and at the end of a turn each beat is exactly the next one at its start. A beat's loudness is half of (1 less the cosine of 2 pi (k + t/T) / L): nothing at the two ends, and the L of them add to L/2 at every moment: so ONE track plays the fastest beat's strokes, each with the summed loudness of the beats it belongs to (`vel_sens 1`: the velocity is the square root). WHAT IT TAUGHT: (1) ALONE THE LAW IS EXACT (every stroke within 0.0 dB of what is written, turn against turn within 0.1). (2) IN THE SONG THE MASTER MOVES SINGLE STROKES 3 TO 4 dB EITHER WAY (strokes written -6 dB or more: -3.0 to +4.4 / -3.9 to +4.3 dB off; the same stroke from turn to turn -3.1 to +1.5 / -4.0 to +1.6): NOT by where in the beat a stroke falls (by sixteenth the middles are -0.1 +0.5 -0.2 +0.7: a 25 ms pip at 1175 Hz is lifted much alike anywhere in the beat here) but stroke by stroke, by what else is sounding. I LEFT IT: a table of 104 trims would mend it in a second build; the fades are slow and the figure is the same every turn within those limits. (3) THE NEW RENDER LESS THE OLD OUTSIDE THE ROW: -91.9 dBFS or less on both seeds: the comparison is fair on both in this song (b/outside.py: run it every round). | A PART LEARNS THE ROOM: when a delay network has been making a rhythm (returns at a, b, a + b ...), give a PLAYED part that rhythm late in the song: one stroke on the source's step and one on every step of the same bar where a return lands, with a pitch per wall. | A ROOM TOO SMALL TO COUNT SINGS (a comb you can tune): two buses that return each other and the source after the same `T` ms ring at `1000 / T` Hz (the source in the middle); `time` must be 6 ms or more, so the top note is 166 Hz (E3); the wall gain sets the ring (`-60 * T / 1000 / seconds` dB); take 0.035 ms off `T` for an eq in the loop (without it the pipes read 4 to 11 cents flat). A source with nothing under 500 Hz still gives the note (its overtones are that far apart). A door bus with a gain lane lets one pipe speak at a time | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t3.py`: `PIPES`, `pipes`, `pipe_buses` | chirper-zero, t3 v4 |
| A SOURCE AS LATE AS IT IS FAR (distance on a click, dry): move the note later by the distance (in sixteenths of sound: 41 m each at 126), turn it down `20 log10(near / (near + distance))` dB with a `gain` fx on a lane, and take its top with the patch's own high `peak` filter (`gain` takes a `mod`: -5 dB a sixteenth round 11 kHz); at distance 0 the patch is what it was, so the rest of the song does not change. CHECK THE MASTER: a sparse click song's loudness gain (here +19 dB) puts every click at the limiter, so a click 14 dB down came out as loud as ever; write the far click that much lower again (`LIMIT`) | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t3.py`: `dist`, `dist_lane`, `click_far` | chirper-zero, t3 v5 |
| A LINE THE ROOM PLAYS (tuned pipes struck by echoes): give each wall of a room that is a rhythm a small room that sings (two buses returning each other after 1000 / Hz ms, ring 0.45 s), tuned to the note a part plays on that wall's answer, and let it hear ONLY that wall; one more, hearing the dry click, for the click's note. The click and its echoes then play the part's line with no part, each note from its own wall. Let the pipes hear a silent copy of the walls fed by the one source (here the click, not the tock), or every other part's echoes strike them too. Then rest the part for a row. COST TO KNOW: pipes ring at the click, where the limiter already works: the rows they sing in came out 0.7 dB quieter | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t3.py`: `sing_buses`, `sing_bars`, `SING_*` | chirper-zero, t3 v6 |
| SOMETHING GOES PAST (Doppler, of a rhythm and a pitch at once): a source on a straight line at speed v (as a part of sound's), d away at its closest, striking a steady pulse on one note. For each stroke struck at s: x = v·(s - s0), r = hypot(d, x), HEARD at s + r (measure distance in sixteenths of sound, so it is also time), pitch 1/(1 + v·x/r) of its own, level d/r, side x/r. Far off the pulse is 1/(1 - v) of the tempo coming and 1/(1 + v) going, and so is the pitch: v = 1/5 gives 5:4 and 5:6, a major third up and a minor third down, a FIFTH between: tune the source so the two heard notes are the key's fifth and its key note. Put s0 on a bar line: every fifth far stroke then falls on the half-bar. Give a bare tone something bright to ride with | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t3.py`: `passes`, `pass_lane`, `PASS_*` | chirper-zero, t3 v7 |
| THE SAME THING PAST AGAIN, FASTER (Doppler as a family of polyrhythms): a source at c/n striking a steady pulse is heard n against n - 1 as it comes and n against n + 1 when it has gone, and its note falls by (n + 1)/(n - 1): c/5 is 5:4 then 5:6 and a fifth; c/3 is 3:2 then 3:4 (half the tempo) and an OCTAVE; c/2 is 2:1 then 2:3 and a twelfth. Choose the source's note so both heard notes are in the key (at c/3: the note a fifth below the one it arrives on). Every lane of it (note, level, side) needs `"curve": 0`: a falling lane is bowed otherwise, and the note reads up to 19 cents flat between strokes | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t3.py`: `AGAIN`, `passes`, `pass_lane`, `STRAIGHT` | chirper-zero, t3 v8 |
| A STANDING WAVE WALKED ACROSS (where you stand is how loud each note is): between walls L apart the modes are n·c/2L; hold a sine on each mode you want and give it the level |cos(n·π·x/L)| at the listener's x, as a dB lane on a `gain` fx (`"curve": 0`, a point a sixteenth, floor -40 dB). Give each EAR its own track (hard left, hard right) at x - 8.5 cm and x + 8.5 cm: high modes then change ears as you walk (their nodes are closer together than the ears) and low ones do not. A note sounds for 95% of its written length (the engine's `play.gate`, default 0.95): give a held patch `play: {gate: 1}`. (CORRECTED 26 Nov 15:50: this row first said a held note "lets go early: write it 5 beats past"; that was the gate, read wrongly. Standing Room v9 wrote its notes 4.5 beats long and they let go half a beat before the wall, as its note says.) | A CLAP COMES BACK AS A CHIRP (a staircase's echo): step n of a flight is `hypot(d + n·tread, n·riser)` from hands d metres off; its echo comes `2r/c` s after the clap. Write EVERY echo as a note on one deterministic tick (a sine with `phase=0`, 5 ms; `vel_sens 0.5` is linear in velocity, `gate 1`), `at` to six decimals of a beat, velocity `(d/r)^1.5`. The echoes are `c/(2·tread)` Hz apart at the foot and `c/(2·hypot(tread, riser))` far up: size the steps for two notes of the key (29.2 and 25.8 cm: D5 to A4). Nearer stairs answer sooner and fall faster. DO NOT use a tick under about 3 ms: the engine moves envelopes every 32 samples (0.67 ms) and a shorter one is a different size and shape each time. Read the note from the line at twice the spacing in 20 ms windows | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t3.py`: `chirp_away`, `stair_echoes`, `chirp_notes`, `CHIRP_*`; patch `sr-step` | chirper-zero, t3 v10 |
| A ROOM THAT FILLS WITH PEOPLE (Sabine: decay is 1 / (1 + people's share)): the engine cannot turn a `returns` gain or a reverb's `decay` while it sounds, so BUILD THE ROOM SEVERAL TIMES (empty, half, full): the same delays, every wall's `returns` gain lower by `3 dB x FULL x share`, the hall's `decay` divided by `1 + FULL x share`; feed the same tracks into all of them and put a lane on each room's door: one room heard at a time, two mixed while people come in (the same delays, so the mix is exact). Anything else the echoes strike (pipes in the walls) needs its own copy per room or it masks the change: read the master where the echoes fall BEFORE publishing. | A CROWD THAT FINDS ONE CLAP (Neda 2000; Kuramoto): a phase a person, `dθ = (ω_i + K·sin(beat's phase − θ))·dt`, a clap each time it comes round. Give each person a place: a row r metres back is `r / 343` s later and `2 m / r` as loud; one track for each place across. Take every "random" number from the golden ratio (`frac(i·φ)`), so the song is the same every time. Read the people ALONE first (the power's own Fourier line at the beat, bar by bar: 0 is nobody, 1 is one click), then in the master read v12 less v11 the same way, and the top of the master in the 33 ms after each beat against the rest of the beat. With D < R the circle goes round your head: nearest R - D behind you, farthest R + D in front, 20 log((R + D) / (R - D)) dB between. The lap's clock is SONG TIME from 0 (phase 0 = far in front, then round by the right): make the lap a whole number of bars and the place repeats to 0.02 ms. Doppler at the most is 1200 log2(c / (c - v D / R)) cents, v = 2 pi R x rate. READ IT BY ARRIVAL: each call's lateness against its written time is its distance (1 ms = 34 cm); which ear first from the two ears' ENVELOPES (a 7 kHz tone's waves repeat every 0.14 ms and cannot say). | AN INTERVAL IS A SPEED (Doppler by the engine's `orbit`, `path: line`): the effect moves the source and lets the time to each ear follow, so a tone f is heard f(1 + v/c) coming and f(1 - v/c) going: (c + v)/(c - v) apart. v = c/n gives (n+1)/(n-1): c/7 a fourth (4/3), c/5 a fifth (3/2), c/3 an octave (2), c/11 a minor third (6/5), c/17 a tone (9/8). To be heard on a NOTE coming, write that note and give the patch's layers `ratio = n/(n+1)`. `rate` = 1/(seconds on the line), `phase` = frac(-t0 x rate) (the effect's clock is song time; a lap starts at the left end), `radius` 20 (its most), `distance` 5: at 2 m the pitch steps in 60 ms and all but the middle is 12 dB down or more, at 5 m it glides through the whole pass; raise the speed by hypot(radius, distance)/radius (3.1%) so the ENDS reach the interval. SET THE FADER IN THE WHOLE SONG'S MASTER: a held tone in bare bars at -18 dB peaked as loud as the click; -30 dB put it 13 under. | SOMETHING FASTER THAN SOUND, HEARD BACKWARDS: a thing on a straight line at M times the speed of sound, d away at its nearest, striking at times `te` (0 = nearest): where it is `x = M c te`, how far `r = hypot(d, x)`, heard at `te + r / c`, and the rate its own time is heard at `1 / (1 + M x / r)`. Coming, `x / r` goes to -1 and the rate to `1 / (1 - M)`: UNDER NOUGHT for M over 1: what it did last is heard first, and every sound it made is heard backwards; at M = 2 the rate is -1, the past at the tempo it happened at. Going, `1 / (1 + M)`. Nothing is heard until `d sqrt(M * M - 1) / (M c)` after it passed. Write each stroke as a note that ENDS on its heard time (a reversed envelope: attack = the length, no decay), length and pitch by the rate, side `x / r`, level `d / r` times the square root of the rate. WHAT IT COST TO LEARN: (1) THE STROKES NEAREST THE PASSING ARE THE WHOLE LEVEL: one stroke 115 m off (-3 dB) heard at 0.41 of its speed came out at 145 Hz nearly as loud as the bass drum (new less old peaked at -2.4 dBFS there; the drum's highest sample is -1.3) when the far ones were 10 to 16 dB under the music in their band: let it rest for the strokes nearest the passing and set the fader by the far ones. (2) A stroke whose ring is folded over the instant of the first arrival (half heard backwards, half forwards, inside a hundredth of a second) cannot be written as one note: leave it out. (3) With every stroke only as loud as it is near, the going bells (low, long) came out 2 to 3 dB over the backward ones written as loud; the same stroke stretched over three times the time has a third of the power, which takes 4.6 dB off them (measured: 2 dB under): that is my reasoning from the energy of one stroke, not a textbook's moving-source formula. Give the wall a lap in sixteenths (`GAL_LAP` 3: 122.5 m at 343 m/s, 39 m across) and the listener a place a fraction `a` of the way round from the source (`GAL_AT` 1/3): the sound arrives from one side at `(k + a)` laps and from the other at `(k + 1 - a)` laps, k = 0 to 6: two tracks panned apart, ONE burst (chip noise with `clock: 0`: the same samples at every note-on, so it IS the same whisper coming round), velocities falling as the square root of the way (3 dB a doubling: the wall's wave spreads along the wall only) and a little a lap. PUT THE SOURCE HALF A SIXTEENTH OFF THE GRID (the source's time is its own; only the lap is the room's): then 31 to 40 of 56 arrivals stand 6 dB over everything else in their band and ear. (2) even so the fall is not delivered as written: arrivals after a bass drum come 6 to 7 dB short. (3) READ A BURST BETWEEN STROKES AGAINST THE MUSIC IN ITS OWN 50 ms, band and ear, with the old music fitted out: 'the mix rises against the 60 ms before' is no use there (the stroke before is louder). | A SOUND HEARD ROUND A CORNER BEFORE IT IS MET (Maekawa's rule; Standing Room v25): the shadow of an edge takes 10 log (3 + 20 N) dB, N = 2 (way round - way through) f / c: about 14 dB of a bass note and 3 dB more for every octave, and only 5 dB of everything at the edge itself, so nearly all the change is in the last metre or two. MAKE IT FROM THE PART'S OWN PARTIALS: read the part alone (one note; its first six partials against its first), write one plain tone a partial (`sine`, the partial as a flat pitch lane), each on its own track at the part's fader plus its partial's level, and put the shadow on a `gain` fx's lane in dB (a point at every note: the shadow at THAT partial's frequency and the fall for the longer way), with the part's own `pump`. WHAT IT COST TO LEARN: (1) CALIBRATE THE PLAIN TONE AGAINST THE PART (alone, same fader: mine read 5.5 dB over the bass's first partial). (2) THE MASTER LIFTS A QUIET LOW PART IN A ROW WITHOUT THE BASS: against the bass as it is met the tones came 0.5 to 1.7 dB over what was written and 3.4 over on the last two notes, and the step at the corner was 1.7 dB for 6.8: 2 dB off the fader put the whole curve within 1.5 dB. SET THE SHADOW AT THE EAR, not in the yaml. (3) under the drum's tail (60 to 100 Hz, -31 dBFS on the offbeat) a tone 22 dB under the bass does not stand: for the first eight bars it is at the tail's level: it is there, not heard as a note. (4) A LOW PART IS THE ADDITION THIS SCORER PAYS FOR in a song short at 114 to 250 Hz: +0.5 of spectrum, though it over-fills 61 and 71 Hz (each a penalty now). | A TAIL THAT GOES DARK (Sabine with the air's term; Standing Room v26): T = 0.161 V / (S a + 4 m V), m from the air's dB a kilometre (ISO 9613-1) over 4343. A band of noise for each octave, each on its own track with a `gain` fx on a dB lane: ONE STRAIGHT LINE from 0 dB at the stroke to -90 dB at 1.5 T (a straight line in dB is an exponential decay, and the engine draws it). WHAT THE BENCH TAUGHT: (1) A BAND'S WALLS MUST BE STEEP: with two filters an edge (24 dB an octave, q 0.7) a slow band's skirt took over a quick neighbour's decay (the 3.6 s band read 5.0 s, the 2.0 s band 3.2 s); with four an edge at Butterworth's q (0.5098, 0.6013, 0.9, 2.5629) they read 3.8 and 2.3 s, and two bands side by side add up level. | A HALL THAT STOPS DYING (Standing Room v33): the engine's reverb has `freeze` (FDN kinds: while it is 0.5 or more the tail is held, with no new input, no decay and no damping); put the hall on the TRACK (`fx`), give the track a lane (`"auto": {"keep": {"points": [...], "curve": 0}}`) and write `"freeze": {"value": 0, "mod": [{"src": "auto.keep", "amount": 1}]}`: the lane's steps (two points 0.001 beat apart) say when it holds and when it lets go. WHAT IT TAUGHT: (1) THE HELD TAIL IS NOT QUITE STILL: alone it falls 2.0 dB in 6.5 beats (3.1 s) and wanders 2 dB by half beat; in the song the master's breathing with the drum makes that 5 dB (-19.2 to -24.5 dBFS). (2) HOLD IT EARLY: held from half a beat after the clap the hall is 2.1 to 3.4 dB under the clap's own half beat (alone); the same hall not held is 23 to 24 dB under it a half beat later (-56/-59, -71/-73, -84/-86, -96/-98 dBFS by half beat, left/right). (3) A WASH IS JUDGED BETWEEN THE STROKES AND BY BAND, AND THE FIRST GUESS WAS 8 dB SHORT: at -15 dB the tail was -31.9 dBFS between the strokes against the old music's -20.3 in its own band (120 to 5000 Hz) and 8.7 dB under it in 700 to 2000 Hz, where the tail is strongest. At -7 dB it is level with the music there (-25.0 against -23.9; over it in 5 / 6 of 13 windows) and still under it below 700 and above 2000 Hz. "Between the strokes" in this row is not empty: the song's clap rings 400 ms and the dub chord's echoes run on. (4) THE MASTER GAVE THE WHOLE 8 dB BACK BUT 0.4 / 0.1 (-31.9 to -24.3; -31.7 to -23.8): a wash at -24 dBFS in a row at -13.7 hardly moves the compressor. (5) LETTING GO ON A BAR THE SCORER CUTS NEAR: all cuts kept their bounds on seed 0; on seed 11 the coarsest cut lost its bound at 18 s (13 sections to 12); recurrence share 0.446 to 0.429 / 0.447 to 0.433: a held part two bars long changes what the row is like. | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t3.py`: `KEPT`, `KEPT_*`, `kept_row`, `kept_times`; `~/.local/state/chirper-zero/c11/t3-v33/b/keptmm.py` (imports the generator once; by half beat the part left / right and the old music in its band; the held tail's highest, lowest and slope; the letting go; between the strokes in the whole band and three parts of it), `b/keptal.yaml` (alone), `b/holdal.yaml` (held and not held, side by side), `ga/` (the first build, -15 dB) | chirper-zero, t3 v33 |
| HOW FAR OFF, TOLD BY THE ROOM (Standing Room v34): ONE patch on TWO tracks: the sound itself, 6 dB less for every doubling of the distance (`vel_sens 1`: velocity 2^(-k/2)) and later by the distance over 343 m/s; and the room's answer, the SAME notes at ONE velocity through a reverb with `mix 1` on the track (nothing of the sound itself comes through: its first 30 ms read -59.4 dBFS against the knock's -32.5). Set the room's fader from a bench of ONE knock: its whole answer's energy against the knock's (here 4.4 dB over, the mean of the two ears; 5.6 and 2.6: the engine's hall answers more on the left), so that they are equal at the distance you choose (the critical distance). WHAT IT TAUGHT: (1) THE MASTER LIFTS THE SAME KNOCK BY PLACE AND BY BAR: a third of a beat after beat 0, 21.2 to 21.6 dB; after beat 2, 16.9 to 19.6: the first build's steps came out -7.8 -4.0 -7.9 -4.2 and, coming back, -1.8 -9.3 -2.6 -4.7 where 6.0 was written. A trim on each knock's velocity (what the first build read, turned over) and one more build: -6.0 -5.9 -5.1 and -6.1 -6.0 -4.2 (the fourth knock is already partly the room). (2) READ THE ROOM AS WIDE AS THE EAR'S OWN FILTER THERE: in 120 to 450 Hz the room after the knock (-39.4 dBFS) is 18 dB under the old music (the bass's 147 and 220 Hz); in 160 to 200 Hz, between those, it is level with it (-40.3 against -41.8: over it in 10 of 16 / 9 of 16): it is NOT plain, and I could not make it so honestly: the room is tied to the knock by the critical distance. (3) A PHYSICAL NUMBER MAY NOT SUIT: by Sabine's rule the hangar's own critical distance is 21 m: the room's answer would be 18 dB lower still. I chose 2.55 m and say so. (4) 16 low strokes at 175 Hz paid the spectrum term 0.19 / 0.18 (182 Hz -6.4 to -6.2: its charge 0.4 to 0.2): the scorer's reading; no cut moved. | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t3.py`: `RECEDE`, `RECEDE_*`, `recede_row`, `recede_knocks`; `~/.local/state/chirper-zero/c11/t3-v34/b/recmm.py` (imports the generator once; every knock's top and the room 130 to 230 ms after it, in 120 to 450 and in 160 to 200 Hz, against the old music in the same windows), `b/energy.py` (one knock against its whole answer), `b/recal.yaml`, `b/onedry.yaml`, `b/onewet.yaml`, `ga/` (the first build, without trims) | chirper-zero, t3 v34 |
| A PLACE MADE BY TIME ALONE, AND BY LEVEL ALONE (Standing Room v35): two tracks of one plain-sine patch (`sine phase=0`: the wave begins afresh with each note, so a later note is the same sound later), pan -1 and pan 1, playing the same notes. BY TIME: the notes' velocities alike; each ear's note begun half the difference to either side of its beat (Woodworth: radius 8.75 cm over 343 m/s, times the angle plus its sine). BY LEVEL: the starts alike; the velocities apart (`vel_sens 1`: dB = 40 log10 of the velocity). WHAT IT TAUGHT: (1) NOTE STARTS ARE ROUNDED TO THE SAMPLE (20.8 millionths of a second at 48 kHz): written -551 -358 -124 +124 +358 +551, read -542 -354 -125 +125 +375 +563 on the bench: within 17, and the two sides not mirror images. (2) PAN -1 IS ONE EAR ONLY: the left track alone leaves the right ear digital silence. (3) THE MASTER KEEPS BOTH: in the finished song, each ear read by itself, the lateness is the bench's within 3 millionths and the ears' difference is the written one within 0.0 dB: the compressor does not pull the two ears apart or together. (4) READ THE OLD MUSIC AT THE PIPS' OWN INSTANTS, EAR BY EAR: the first build began each walk in the first bar of its four and its second pip (beat 2.333 of bars 145 and 149) was 4 to 7 dB UNDER the old music in 300 to 640 Hz (the twelfths reading of the whole row had said -64 dBFS there: it is an average over eight bars, and those two bars are not like the others). The second build leaves the first bar of each four out: the quieter ear is over the old music by 10.5 dB at the least. (5) ON SEED 0 THE NEW RENDER IS NOT THE OLD ONE PLUS THE PART: with two tracks more, rows far from the part differ (the new less the old: bars 1-8 -49.0 dBFS, bars 81-88 -44.5, bars 161-168 -66.0; on seed 11 -86.8 or less everywhere outside the row), though v34 rendered twice on seed 0 is the same to the sample. So seed 0's score here (+0.021) is not the part's; seed 11's (-0.002) is. Why: not found (the song has 14 patches with noise in them). COMPARE THE OLD AND THE NEW OUTSIDE THE PART'S ROW ON BOTH SEEDS BEFORE BELIEVING A SCORE MOVE. | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t3.py`: `EARS`, `EARS_*`, `ears_row`, `ears_pips`; `~/.local/state/chirper-zero/c11/t3-v35/b/earmm.py` (imports the generator once; each ear by itself: level, the right less the left, the left ear's lateness by cross-correlation with a parabola between samples; the old music in each ear at each pip), `b/earl.yaml`, `b/earb.yaml`, `outside.txt`, `seed0.txt`, `ga/` (the first build: eight places from the first bar) | chirper-zero, t3 v35 |
| HEIGHT THAT IS NOT IN THE SOUND (Standing Room v36): one plain pip, pan 0, no room, the same in both ears: only its note moves, up or down a ladder; a higher note is said to seem to come from higher up (Pratt). WHAT TO MEASURE: that nothing else moves: the two ears' difference in level and in time (b/rungmm.py: each ear by itself, the phase between them), the note against what is written, the level pip by pip. WHAT IT TAUGHT: (1) READ THE OLD MUSIC AT EVERY ONE OF THE PART'S OWN INSTANTS, IN THE BAND OF THE NOTE IT PLAYS THERE, BEFORE THE FIRST BUILD (b/oldat.py). I read the row by sixteenth (the emptiest: -56 to -79 dBFS) and the first build still put one pip 2.5 dB UNDER the old music and six more less than 10 dB over it: the thing that goes past is nearest in bar 157, where the ladder had its top, and the old music under the second pip was -25.6 dBFS. oldat.py tried both ways round and three places: DOWN THEN UP with the pips of beats 1 and 3 three sixteenths after the beat has nothing louder than -38.5 dBFS under any pip; the build then read 12.1 / 11.9 dB over at the least. (2) THE MASTER'S LIFT IS AT ITS CEILING IN THIS ROW: every pip a sixteenth after its beat came out alike (-28.1 dBFS, a lift of 21.9 dB) but the one at the thing's nearest (1.2 dB less): a trim of 1.2 dB on all the others made that place alike within 0.1 dB. | THE WAY DOWN LEADS UP (Risset's paradox: a scale that falls and ends higher): bells with no octave step DOWN round notes that divide the octave evenly (three semitones a step), while the curve every bell is under climbs slowly (an octave over 48 steps: a quarter of a semitone a step, far less than the step). When the steps are a multiple of the notes, the last bell is the first one's name with its weight an octave up. Say it: the first bell alone before, then last, first, last, first. To claim it, the two must be equal IN THE MASTER, and the master is not even-handed: (1) whatever falls with the bass drum loses 8.5 dB; (2) a click a sixteenth after the song's click and bass loses 3 to 6 dB against one a sixteenth after the drum. So: render them written the same, read new less old over 1.5 kHz in the 25 ms after each stroke, section by section (`twom.py`), write the second up by what it lost, render again and read again (here 0.0, -2.0, -4.0, -6.0 dB against a plan of 0, 2, 4, 6, on both seeds). | WHAT THE SCORER'S RHYTHM TERM SEES (tools/compscore.py `m_rhythm`; likeness.py `Feat.onset`): the onset curve is the sum over 40 log bands (30 Hz to 16 kHz) of every band's RISE in dB from one frame to the next (21 ms apart; an 85 ms window, so an onset shows three frames early). A click after silence is worth 1200 to 1600; a quiet click in a clean gap nearly as much; a drum under other parts next to nothing. The off-grid share is the squared heights of the autocorrelation's peaks off the eighth grid: only strokes on ODD SIXTEENTHS make them (half sixteenths fall inside the on-grid peaks and count for nothing), and four a bar at half the click's height over 120 bars were worth 0.03. | THE OCTAVE ILLUSION (Deutsch 1974): two plain tones an octave apart, a quarter of a second each with no gap (an eighth at 125: 240 ms); one ear is given LOW HIGH LOW HIGH, the other HIGH LOW HIGH LOW, as loud as each other: each ear has both notes and the ears never the same one. Most people hear one note at a time, the high in one ear and the low in the other, and it does not move when the headphones are turned round. SHOW IT THE WAY THE SCALES ARE SHOWN: one ear's part alone, then the other's, then both. Headphones only; in one speaker it is a held octave. WHAT IT COST TO LEARN: (1) in a full passage the master reads a HELD tone 8.7 dB over its fader figure (a pip of the same patch level reads near its figure): at -26 dB two tones were 6 dB under ALL the music of the row and took 0.6 dB off it.
