(2) A TONE IN A BAND THE SONG IS ALREADY OVER IN COSTS NEARLY THE SAME AT ANY LEVEL (468 and 877 Hz: spectrum -1.5 at -20 dB, -1.1 at -26, -0.8 at -34): the other side of "coverage mends a short band at any level" | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t5.py`: `oct_span`, `oct_ear`, `oct_notes`, `OCT_*`; `~/.local/state/chirper-zero/c11/t5-v18/b/octmm.py` (in the master: eighth by eighth and ear by ear) | chirper-zero, t5 v18 |
| A BELL WHOSE OCTAVE IS A STEP DOWN (Risset 1986, after Schroeder): give a bell tones S times apart, S a little more than 2, under ONE FIXED CURVE (as the Shepard bells have theirs 2 apart). Written higher by a ratio R, every tone lands `1200 * log2(R / S)` cents from the old bell's next tone and the ear follows it there: R = 2, S = 2.1: 84.5 cents DOWN for every octave written up (S = 2.1189 would make it a semitone exactly; S = 2 is Shepard's bell, which does not move at all). One patch a written note (the levels are by Hz, not by layer); leave out tones under a floor so every bell has the same number of them. WHAT IT COST TO LEARN: (1) CHOOSE THE CURVE'S PLACE BY `--bands`: this song is over the album from 292 to 1922 Hz; with the curve at 2900 Hz, 0.8 octaves wide (tones at 1.1-1.5, 2.2-3.1 and 4.7-6.6 kHz) the spectrum term did not move at either level tried. (2) a floor of -30 dB let four of the eight bells have a tone at 10.4 to 12 kHz; -20 keeps three tones each and nothing over 10 kHz. (3) THE MASTER PAYS FOR A NEW BAND OUT OF THE OLD ONES: the bells put 14 dB more between 2.1 and 3.4 kHz in their row, and the master took 0.3 dB off everything else there (the row finished 0.2 dB QUIETER than without them). (4) SAY WHAT IT IS: under a fixed curve the bell written an octave up is, tone for tone, the bell 84.5 cents down (the weights differ by 2 dB at most): the paradox is in how it is written, as Shepard's is. | A VOICE OUT OF ONE NOTE (McAdams 1984; Chowning's Phone): hold a note made of plain tones, the multiples 1 to N of one frequency, EACH ON ITS OWN TRACK, and add nothing. Give every n-th tone the SAME small vibrato (here 0.35 semitones each way, 5.5 a second, through a pitch lane) and leave the rest still: the ones that move together are heard as a second voice on n times the note, though no tone is louder than it was. Change n by the bar and the voice sings: n = 3 4 6 4 3 2 on D2 is A3 D4 A4 D4 A3 D3. WHAT IT COST TO LEARN: (1) THE TILT IS THE WHOLE LEVEL QUESTION. At 6 dB an octave the fundamental was 29 dB over everything else in its band and the note as loud as the rest of the row, while the tones that have to be heard wavering were 12 dB under to 2 over the music in their bands: 3 dB an octave and 8 dB less at the bottom gives the same voice without the boom. (4) TO READ A TONE'S SWING IN A FINISHED MASTER take new less k times old first, then a band of the same width IN HZ round every tone (0.42 of the fundamental each side): a band 5% each side lets the next tone's swing in from the twelfth tone up and reads tones as wavering that are not. | TWO LINES THAT CROSS AND ARE HEARD TO TURN BACK (bouncing glides): two plain tones in ONE place, the same level, one straight up and one straight down through the same two octaves, crossing half-way; the ear joins each to what is nearest in pitch after the crossing and hears a U and an arch. Then the same again with one of them given a quality of its own (a flutter): now it is heard to go through. WHAT IT COST TO LEARN: (1) A LANE WITHOUT `curve: 0` IS NOT A STRAIGHT LINE WHEN IT FALLS: the rising tone was straight to 1.5 cents and the falling one, written the same way, was 805 cents UNDER its line at the middle (it fell fast and then slowly): the two did not cross where written. Read both lines' pitch on a bench before anything else. (2) two plain tones crossing INTERFERE for 60 ms either side: in step they touch (3 dB up), out of step they leave a hole 25 dB deep. The sum (609.4 waves to the crossing) gave the wrong start; a bench of eight starts gave it: set it by measurement. (3) a crossing ON a bar line is ducked by the bass drum straight after it (5 dB for 50 ms): the touch is heard before the line, not on it. | A RHYTHM HEARD IN NOISE THAT HAS NONE (repeated noise: Guttman and Julesz, 1963; and a hiss that is the same on every seed): the engine's chip noise with `clock: 0` is a shift register of 32767 steps, 93 steps to a turn of the note played, begun from the same state at every note-on: it is EXACTLY periodic (alone: 0.9996 to 1.0000 like itself one loop later, to the sample) and identical on every seed. Tune it so that it comes round every L sixteenths: `hz = 32767 / (93 * L * sixteenth in seconds)`; lengths an octave apart share one patch (`pitch: {value: semitones from the nearest note}`). Hold it (gate 1, sustained), band it, and set it beside a hiss that never repeats (`white`) in the same band at the same level: the difference between them is only that one comes round. WHAT IT TAUGHT: (1) EQUAL IS KEPT WITHIN A CHORD, NOT BETWEEN CHORDS: in the master the eight tones of one whole chord lie 0.2 dB apart (0.3 at most) and the tone that was left out is +0.00 dB against the others (-0.07 to +0.13); but the sixteen chords come out up to 3.3 dB apart (the Risset hat ducks some), and a chord's second half is 5 to 9 dB under its first (the click and the bass fall in its middle): say "no louder than the rest", not "the same every time". | ONE NOTE, LOUD AND SOFT, FOR A PITCH THAT IS NOT WRITTEN (Hearing Things v31): a plain low tone on two tracks 12 dB apart, a note every two beats by turns, then one held note under a lane in dB (`fx: gain 0dB + auto.heavy`, `curve 0`): alone every note reads the same frequency to 0.01 cents and the two levels 12.0 dB apart. WHAT IT TAUGHT: (1) THIS SONG IS FULL: PRINT A TABLE OF ALL ITS ROWS FIRST (tracks, low notes, the old music by band and by sixteenth): five plans were dropped on reading it: a loud low note in bars 113-120 (the falling bass of v26 is there, at -50 dBFS in that band), in bars 9-16 (it would fill the empty beats of the filled beat), in bars 25-32 (the two scales are taught there), an arpeggio at A3 in bars 65-80 (200 to 350 Hz is at -17 and -20 dBFS on two sixteenths of every four), and a note under the drum anywhere (the drum is on every beat and the bass on every off-beat below 180 Hz). | A SWELL WRITTEN AS LENGTHS (Hearing Things v32): one pip at one height, held 5, 10, 20, 40, 80 ms and back: every doubling is 3 dB more sound at the same top, and under about 200 ms the ear adds it up. | THE TUNE TWO LOOPS MAKE (a resulting pattern, after Reich): give each stroke of a pattern a note (the notes of one chord); when a second copy of the pattern is k steps ahead, a pitched voice plays only the steps on which both strike (`doubles(k)`). Every phase then has its own tune, each a piece of the chord, and the two together are the whole chord. For a copy that never stops, play a phase's tune only in the bars where the copy is within a quarter of a step of it | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t7.py`: `TUNE`, `doubles`, `tune_lock`, `tune_notes` | chirper-zero, t7 v3 |

The ear hears one click, at the side that is first. Turn both down 3 dB while they stand apart (two clicks an ear each are then as loud as one in the middle). On one speaker it is a comb with its first hollow at `1 / (2 x lead)`: 833 Hz at 0.6 ms. | A BASS NOBODY WROTE (the form picks the note): when two loops of one pattern lock, the first stroke they share has a pitch; give the bass that pitch for as long as the lock holds or is nearest. Here it is G or B flat and the line is G, B flat, G, B flat, G, B flat, G as one loop drifts past seven locks. A beating bass (two sines a fixed number of Hz apart) needs ONE PATCH A NOTE, since the ratio is the note's own. | A TUNE IN CANON WITH ITSELF (pitched phase, Piano Phase's way): give a short looping tune a second voice with the same notes (an octave off, so the two are told apart) whose phase x -> x + phase(x) steps a sixteenth every few bars, creeping for the first part of each (u squared) and then caught. Each lock is a new composite: interleaved where the loops' strokes miss, dyads where they meet. Build the tune from one chord and every lock is consonant. | A LOCK AS A PICTURE (the precedence effect, drawn by a phase process): two parts with the SAME deterministic click, one to each side. Where they strike together the two copies are one click in the middle; alone they are at the sides; as one slips ahead the middle click leans to the leader (under 1 ms), sits on its side (1 to 5 ms), widens, splits. The pan is a balance law (a part at p gives its far ear 1 - p), so turn each part up by (2 - p0)/(2 - p) when it moves from p0 to p: the two ears added are then what they were. A send is taken AFTER the track's gain and pan (corrected 26 Nov 15:00: I had written BEFORE): the bus gets 2 - p of a part at p, so the turn-up above also keeps the room what it was: leave the send. Put the moved bars on tracks of their own (static pan and gain; no lanes). Expect the master's compressor to answer the louder single-ear clicks by a few tenths of a dB | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t7.py`: `shape_span`, `shape_gain`, `eared`, `SHAPE_*` | chirper-zero, t7 v7 |
| HALF A WAVE OUT OF STEP (a comb, tuned to the click): two copies of one DETERMINISTIC pitched click (a saw or sines with `phase=0`; not the machine's rim) at the SAME pan, the second early by half a period of the click's note (500/f ms): odd harmonics cancel (27 dB here; the decay is what is left), even ones stay, and the click is heard an octave up and 3 dB quieter; early by a whole period (1000/f ms) it is itself again. One number makes a rhythm (sixteenths), a place (under a ms, an ear each) or a note (half a period, one place). Keep the two in one place: at pans -p and +p each ear gets 1 and 1 - p of them and the notch is only (2 - p)/p deep. Expect the master to come UP a few tenths where the click is thinner | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t7.py`: `wave_span`, `wave_ms`, `wave_gain`, `mid`, `WAVE_*` | chirper-zero, t7 v8 |
| A SCALE FROM ONE CLICK (the harmonic series by timing alone): strike one DETERMINISTIC pitched click (a saw with `phase=0`) n times, each `1000 / (n·f)` ms after the last, on n tracks at one pan and one gain: the harmonics that are not multiples of n cancel and the click is heard on n·f, no higher in peak than one copy. n = 2, 3, 4, 6, 8 are octaves and fifths; 5 is a major third, 7 the natural seventh, 9 the ninth. Write `at` to five decimals of a beat. Bench the tracks alone: read the strongest harmonic of f in each stroke and the ones that should cancel against the ones that should not (12 to 16 dB here, limited by the click's decay and whole-sample starts). A high-pass in the patch makes the low rungs quieter: say so, or trim | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t7.py`: `scale_span`, `scale_strokes`, `scale_notes`, `SCALE_*` | chirper-zero, t7 v9 |

| THREE AGAINST TWO FROM FOUR SINES (a polyrhythm of pumps; beats as rhythm): a pair of sines `r` cycles a beat apart, both `phase=0` and equally loud, struck on an off-beat, swells every `1/r` beats from the stroke and dies half-way between. Put a pair at `r = 1` (once a beat) over a pair at `r = 1.5` a fourth or fifth away: 3 against 2, agreeing once every two beats. Any `p/q` works: `r = 4/3` is 4 against 3 over a bar. Keep each pair's MEAN in tune (`ratio = k * sharp -/+ r * BPM / 120 / f0`). Read it with the size of the analytic signal in each pair's band, smoothed 40 ms (an RMS window ripples at bass notes and gives wrong beats). LEVEL IT IN THE MASTER, 6 dB under the bass's fader to begin with, and end its last note on one of its own dips. | A COMB FROM TWO NOTES (a flanger with no effect): the SAME noiseless click on two tracks in the same place, the second `n / 48000` s later (note times to 7 places of a beat: starts are exact to the sample). They cancel at `24000 / n` Hz times 1, 3, 5 ...; sweep n = 0 .. 12 .. 0 on a cosine over four bars and the notches walk down the undertones of 24 kHz and back. In step (n = 0) it is one click, 6 dB up: use that for the sections where the song locks. Read alone with one click a channel (lag to the sample, how alike), then in one place: each line of the click against the same line in step, against the comb's curve. In the master read v12 less v11 the same way. A SHORT BAND NEEDS COVERAGE, NOT LEVEL: the scorer's band is a mean of dB over frames, so the same part over 86 bars for 48 took the 15 kHz band from -12.6 to -10.9 dB | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t7.py`: `comb_span`, `comb_apart`, `comb_strokes`, `comb_notes`, `COMB_*`; patch `os-air` | chirper-zero, t7 v12 |

Two things follow, both measured on window renders (`wdens.py`). (1) YOU CANNOT ADD ONSETS TO A SECTION THAT HAS ONE ON EVERY SIXTEENTH: a click between the sixteenths, bright or dull, 7.3 to 11.5 dB under the music, took the count DOWN (8.74 a second to 8.2-8.4). (2) A HELD SOUND TAKES THEM AWAY: under a held note the clicks no longer rise out of nothing. On window renders of the eight bars: a saw 13.9 dB under the music, 8.5 to 6.2 a second; 7.7 dB under, 2.8; pink noise 13 dB under, 1.7. In the published song (9.6 dB under) the scorer's own section reads 5.0 for 8.4. So a break with a held sound reads as a bare section, and `density_cv` went 0.15 to 0.19 (variety 90.7 to 98.2). Give each pair its own Δ in cycles a beat (1, 1.5, 2.5 -> 2, 3, 5 swells in two beats) and strike all on the same off-beat: they agree there and every two beats and nowhere between. Keep every pair's MEAN as sharp as the first's so the intervals between pairs are true. One note a bar (the phases start again each bar); let the last go where the pair has just died (`(floor((BEATS - 0.5) * rate - 0.5) + 0.5) / rate` beats after the stroke). | A TERM ON ITS FLOOR TIPS ON ANYTHING (read it by cut before designing): Out of Step's recurrence share is 0.274 against a floor of 0.25 (`RETURN` 0.45-0.75: only matches over 0.45 count; three sections carry it: 0.54, 0.55, 0.94). Use a sound whose band is otherwise EMPTY in those bars (here nothing else is over 10 kHz), and set the fader in the whole song's master (-18 dB here: 12 dB under the lead's peaks; at -12 it peaked with the lead). STOP IT A ROW BEFORE THE NEXT PART THAT TAKES THE SAME BAND: run right up to the hat, the hat's entry stopped being a cut for the scorer (8 sections to 7) and arrived on a top already ticking. | A CLICK WITH A NOTE (the phase vocoder's robot, `pvoc mode=robot`): keep each frequency's strength and set every phase to nought in every window: the spectrum is the click's (measured: 1.4 dB in every octave, 0.2 dB overall) and the sound now repeats at the window rate, so it has a pitch. Drive the pitch from a silent key track (`tune: {mod: [{src: track.KEY.key, amount: 12}]}`; `pitch` is the note sung for a key of C4). TWO THINGS TO KNOW: (1) the effect works 1024 samples (21 ms) ahead of what is heard, so SET THE KEY BEFORE THE STROKE (0.1 of a beat here): set on the stroke, each click sings the previous note; (2) the robot's note stops at 750 Hz: sing an octave down (`pitch: C3`) if the tune goes over F#5. The first stroke starts 9 ms before its written time (the window's reach). SET THE FADER IN THE WHOLE SONG'S MASTER: at -6 dB the sung click peaked as high as the click; -12 dB put it 3.5 to 9 dB under. A click is short: only 4 to 9 periods of the note fit in it | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t7.py`: `sing_span`, `sing_keys`, `SING_*`; `~/.local/state/chirper-zero/c11/t7-v16/b/singm.py` (alone), `singmm.py` (in the master) | chirper-zero, t7 v16 |
| A PULSE MADE OF HELD TONES (Fourier's drum; pulse compression): a beat-rate pulse is the sum of the multiples of the beat's frequency (BPM / 60) all in phase on the beat. Hold n of them in the band wanted (`sine phase=... ratio=...`, taper the weights or the thump rings before and after), and nothing is struck. QUADRATIC PHASE spreads it: tone k out by `k^2 / (2 (n - 1))` of a turn lays the thump over exactly one beat as a falling slide, at the SAME level and spectrum. Detune tone k by `-phase_k / T` Hz and they are back in phase T seconds later: a hum that draws itself into a drum on a chosen bar line. Notes start 2 ms late: so do the thumps. WHAT IT COST TO LEARN: (1) THE SONG'S MASTER LIFTS A HELD LOW PART ABOUT 20 dB over a render of it alone (the same as t3's hum): start 20 dB under the first guess (-28 dB was louder than the row; -54 was right). | A CROWD THAT FINDS THE BEAT WITHOUT BEING TOLD (Kuramoto 1975, with Ermentrout's fireflies, 1991): N clicks start together; click i runs at its own tempo (evenly from -D to +D); after some deaf beats each one LEANS towards the rest by the mean sine of how far ahead of it they are (`lead' = rate + lean * mean sin`) and LEARNS its tempo by the same (`rate' = learn * mean sin`); a stroke wherever a click's own phase passes a whole turn. Step it in the generator (midpoint rule, 192 steps a beat: 768 notes cost nothing) and write the strokes as notes. WHAT MAKES IT WORK: (1) NOBODY IS TOLD THE BEAT: with the tempos spread evenly about the song's and one start for all, the swarm is its own mirror image, so its mean phase stays on the grid and that is where it gathers. (2) THE OFF-BEAT TRAP: once the fan is wider than half a beat each side the swarm's mean points at the OFF-beat, and that is where they gather (seen twice, with a pull too weak to stop the fan in time: all twelve ended 238 ms from the beat). Pull hard enough, soon enough, that the fan never gets there (here it stops at 0.39 of a beat each side). (3) LEANING ALONE NEVER ARRIVES: each stays arcsin(its offset / the lean) out for ever (by the sum's own arithmetic); learning the tempo takes that to nought (here 0.01 ms by beat 24). (4) some pairs of lean and learn send the outermost round the long way (the slowest loses a stroke, the fastest gains two: seen at 5% / 0.24 / 0.06 and at 4% / 0.6 / 0.25): print how many strokes each has and try again; here the pull rises from nought. (5) GIVE EACH ITS OWN NOTE: in step they then add as power (11 dB over one), not as amplitude (22 dB). (6) IN THE MASTER a lone tick stands 19 dB over a render alone; the same twelve IN STEP ON THE BEAT, with the song's click, come out 6 dB under what they are apart: the fan is the loud part | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t7.py`: `swarm_ticks`, `SWARM_*`; `~/.local/state/chirper-zero/c11/t7-v18/b/sim.py` (the arithmetic alone: try N, D, deaf, lean, learn), `b/swarmmm.py` (the fan beat by beat in the master) | chirper-zero, t7 v18 |
| A SECOND DRUM THAT CHANGES GEAR (every small ratio, in order): take every ratio p:q from 1:1 to 2:1 with q no more than N, sorted (the Farey sequence of order N; `fractions.Fraction` and a set). A turn of p:q is q beats with p strokes at `j * q / p`; hold each gear for `ceil(beats in a bar / q)` whole turns. For N = 4 in four-four that is 4 + 4 + 6 + 4 + 6 + 4 + 4 = 32 beats: eight bars exactly, and every change is on a stroke the two drums share. WHAT IT COST TO LEARN: (1) THE BASS DRUM SWALLOWS WHAT FALLS WITH IT: the stroke at the start of each turn, written loudest (velocity 1), is delivered 3 to 14 dB UNDER the strokes between (velocity 0.72): the master holds the drum's instant down (2 to 6 dB over a render of the stroke alone) and lifts the gaps (9 to 20 dB over). Do not fight it: the bass drum is the turn's mark; set the fader by the strokes between. (2) so 1:1 adds nothing that is heard as a drum (it is all with the bass drum), and of 2:1 only the off-beat strokes are heard, under the bass's own swell there. The gears worth hearing are the five between. (3) TO READ A LOW DRUM'S TIME IN A MASTER, match what is left (new less k * old, 100 to 450 Hz) against a render of one stroke alone, and look only within half a turn of its own wave: with a wider window the match lands a whole turn (7.6 ms) late on the strokes the bass drum has reshaped. | A CLICK MADE OF PHASE ALONE (a held tone turned upside down): multiply a plain tone by a square of plus and minus one (`ringmod` from a layer with `audible: false`); every change of side is a click, a step of 2 x the tone's height x the sine of where in its wave it was caught, so (N + 1/16) waves to a turn gives an accent that comes round every 8 turns with nobody playing it. WHAT IT COST TO LEARN: (1) A TURN IS 12 dB UNDER ITS OWN TONE (6 ms over 1 kHz; a step falls 6 dB an octave): heard plain, the tone must be the loudest thing in the row before its turns are heard (refused: 11 dB over the music in its band and the turns still 6 dB under the clicks). HEAR THE TRACK THROUGH A SHELF where the tone has nothing (+12 dB over 600 Hz): the tone is untouched and the turns come up 12 dB. Say so in the song's text. (2) through the shelf the CORNER a turn leaves counts with its step: the cycle is not the plain sine written (0.20 0.56 0.83 0.98 0.98 0.83 0.56 0.20) but 0.07 0.18 0.60 0.85 0.67 0.29 0.54 0.15, the same in every cycle and on both seeds: read it, do not assume it. (3) PUT THE TURNS BETWEEN THE SIXTEENTHS (the square's `phase: 0.25` starts it a quarter of its cycle in): on them each fell with the song's clicks. (4) TWO NOTES END TO END DO NOT MAKE THE CLICK: this engine smooths a note's start and end (70.5 waves to a note gave -44 dBFS over 2 kHz where the ring modulator gives -20). (5) THIS ENGINE'S SLOW OSCILLATORS RUN LATE: their phase is summed in single precision; at 1.4 Hz the step is 489.3 parts in 2^24 and is rounded to 489 in the upper half of the cycle, which lasts 0.245 ms too long (the lower 0.04 too short): 0.1 ms a turn, measured, and three other frequencies agreed with the sum (1.40039: 0.062; 1.4002: 0.072; 1.4006: 0.24 EARLY, the step rounds to 490). Tune the tone to the turn AS MADE (`TURN_SLOW`), or the accent's cycle is 6.1 turns, not 8. (6) an alone bench near full scale squeezes the large steps (the bench has a master too): read shares at a low fader. | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t7.py`: `turn_span`, `turn_hz`, `turn_steps`, `TURN_*`; `~/.local/state/chirper-zero/c11/t7-v20/b/turnmm.py` (imports the generator; the note and every turn in the master), `b/turnal2.py` + `b/turn2.yaml` (alone, with and without the shelf), `b/flip3.yaml` (the slow square at four frequencies) | chirper-zero, t7 v20 |
| A REAL PENDULUM PUSHED OVER THE TOP (swinging becomes going round; the swing that all but reaches the top hangs): work the motion out in the generator: angle'' = -(2 pi / T)^2 sin(angle), stepped 960 times a beat (leapfrog: half a kick, a drift, half a kick), and at every pass through the bottom (the angle crosses a whole number of turns) multiply the speed by a fixed push and write the escapement's tick there. Choose the push so that after N pushes the swing reaches a chosen angle just short of the top: push = (sin(near / 2) / sin(start / 2)) ** (1 / N); 176.8 degrees gives a hang of 6.3 beats where a small swing takes 2. Write WHERE THE BOB IS as a small tick evenly in time on ONE track whose pan is a lane (`"pan": {"value": 0, "mod": [{"src": "auto.NAME", "amount": 1}]}`, a point at every tick, `curve: 0`): pan = sin(angle), level by height. WHAT IT COST TO LEARN: (1) the pan lane is followed tick by tick: alone, right less left was within 2.3 dB of the equal-power law, tan((pan + 1) pi / 4), for all 256 ticks: one track does what nine fixed-pan tracks did for the corners. (2) put the ticks half a gap off the grid (here on the odd sixty-fourths): none falls with a stroke. (3) READ THE SCORER'S "A SHORT SECTION CARRIES A FORM TERM" LINE BEFORE CHOOSING THE ROW: the row before this one's break is where a 1-point form term hangs. (4) no new patch: the bob is the swarm's 1.5 ms tick on another note. | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t7.py`: `over_row`, `over_path`, `over_bob`, `OVER_*`; `~/.local/state/chirper-zero/c11/t7-v21/b/overmm.py` (imports the generator; every tick of the bob in the master: over the music or not, by height, its side against the written pan; every tick of the escapement), `b/overal.py` and `b/over.yaml` (the row alone: pan against the law) | chirper-zero, t7 v21 |
| A RHYTHMICON (Cowell and Theremin, 1931: one note comes apart into its harmonics, the n-th struck n times a bar): N tracks, each one plain tone at n times one note (`sine phase=0 ratio=n`, the same written note on every track), strokes at `bar + j / n` for j = 0 .. n - 1, the n-th fader 20 log10(n) dB under the first (a saw's law). ON THE BAR LINE all strike from phase nought and are the first N terms of a saw: one note (alone: 0.984 like it). INSIDE THE BAR they are 1 : 2 : 3 : ... : N at once, and the harmonics striking at p/q of the bar (lowest terms) are q, 2q, 3q ...: the harmonics of a note q times as high: the note at any moment is the DENOMINATOR of the time (Farey's fractions as a tune: `rhy_together`). Bring them in low to high (two a bar here) so the ear is given the series before the tangle. CHOOSE THE ROOT SO THAT THE SERIES IS THE SONG'S CHORD: 1 to 6 and 8 are root, fifth and major third: on the relative major of a minor song (B flat over G) they are its minor seventh chord; the seventh harmonic is 31 cents under the minor seventh and is nobody's note. WHAT IT COST TO LEARN: (1) THE SAW'S LAW STARVES THE TOP: the eighth is 18 dB under the first and was the one to set the fader by (from the quiet end). (2) a harmonic that is already somebody's note (the bass's root, the tune's third) is under that part at any fader: it is heard as more of that note. (3) one harmonic a bar leaves the last one a single bar: two a bar, then the full set for half the row. (4) eight tones in the middle of the spectrum cost this song 0.01 at the bottom (the scorer's mean), at any of three faders. | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t7.py`: `rhy_row`, `rhy_strokes`, `rhy_together`, `RHY_*`; `~/.local/state/chirper-zero/c11/t7-v22/b/rhymm.py` (imports the generator: every stroke in its own band in the master, by harmonic, on and off the beat), `b/rhyal.py` and `b/rhy.yaml` (alone: each harmonic's frequency and level, and the bar line against a saw) | chirper-zero, t7 v22 |
| THE SAME SPECTRUM, ANOTHER SOUND (phase only: a click through a chain of all-pass filters is a chirp; Out of Step v23): put k `filter kind=allpass` effects on a BUS, their frequencies evenly spaced in pitch from `lo` to `hi` (q 2), and send a click into it (`out: bus`). Every filter turns the phase once round near its own frequency, so a frequency f is held back about `k / (f ln(hi / lo))` seconds (exactly: the sum over the filters of `2 / (q w0) (1 + x^2) / ((1 - x^2)^2 + x^2 / q^2)`, x = f / f0): the low ones last: a falling chirp. 256 filters from 400 Hz to 8 kHz hold 588 Hz back 104 ms and 4.7 kHz 13. Double k to double the chirp. WHAT IT COST TO LEARN: (1) THE ENGINE STOPS A BUS'S EFFECTS WHEN THE LAST VOICE FEEDING IT ENDS: a 27 ms click through 256 filters came out 25 ms long with its 3rd harmonic 30 dB down. HOLD A NOTE 120 dB DOWN INTO THE BUS for as long as its output lasts (`os-keep`), and READ THE ENERGY AND THE BANDS AGAINST THE DRY SOUND ON A BENCH: an all-pass chain must leave every band within 0.1 dB. (2) the chirp's PEAK is 14 to 21 dB under the click's with the same energy, AND THIS MASTER HOLDS PEAKS: it delivered the copy 7.6 dB louder through 256 filters than through 32 at one fader. (3) under 64 filters (29 ms) the copy is a click on a click: what is heard is the TAIL after the dry click is over: read the mix against the old there (500 to 1500 Hz, 30 to 110 ms after: 0, 5, 7, 8 dB by stage), not the stroke. (4) read each harmonic's arrival as the middle of its band's energy against the dry click's: within 10% of the formula. (5) it costs 0.002 (spectrum: 1200 Hz was 0.7 under its edge). NOT DONE: the backward chirp, which the same chain would give back as a click. | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t7.py`: `disp_row`, `disp_cuts`, `disp_late`, `disp_notes`, `DISP_*`; `~/.local/state/chirper-zero/c11/t7-v23/b/dispmm.py` (imports the generator: each stage in the master: level, each harmonic's arrival, length, the tail against the old mix), `b/dispal.py` and `b/disp.yaml` (alone: energy and six bands against the dry click, arrivals, length, peak) | chirper-zero, t7 v23 |
| A WHISTLE IN, A CLICK OUT (pulse compression; Out of Step v24): take the chain of all-pass filters that makes a click a chirp (v23) and feed it the chirp BACKWARDS: one plain tone (`sine`, held, `gate 1`) whose pitch lane sounds each frequency f at `T - late(f)`, where `late(f)` is the chain's delay there (the generator's `disp_late(f, k)`: the sum of the filters' group delays): 81 points evenly spaced in pitch, low to high, `curve: 0`. Send it into the chain (`out: bus`) and a click comes out at T. THE ENGINE FOLLOWS THE LANE WELL ENOUGH: 107 ms of whistle came out 1.4 ms long (5% to 95% of its energy), 53 ms 1.2, 27 ms 1.1, 13 ms 1.0, peaking 1.9 ms after T. A plain tone of constant level swept this way has the spectrum of a saw's edge (energy as 1 over f squared), so the click is like the song's own: its octave bands came within 2.2 dB of A's click's with nothing done. THE SAME ENERGY IN GIVES THE SAME CLICK OUT: a whistle half as long goes in 3 dB up. WHAT IT COST TO LEARN: (1) KEEP THE BUS RUNNING (`os-keep`, -120 dB, from a beat before): v23's trap. (2) the click's level is the whistle's ENERGY: at -28 dB into 256 filters it had A's own click's energy to 0.3 dB: start from there, not from the tone's fader. (3) THIS MASTER GIVES 2 dB FOR 6 dB OF FADER ON A CLICK (it holds peaks): set the level by the row's push (the old music fitted 0.26 dB down at -28, 0.16 at -34), not by the click. (4) put the click HALF A SIXTEENTH OFF the song's strokes (t3 v24): 16 of 16 stood. (5) the whistle HEARD going in wants ONE fader for all lengths: 3 dB up a halving put the short ones 10 dB over the music. (6) a bench render clips at -1 dBFS: a compressed pulse read there loses energy to the clip: bench it low. | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t7.py`: `back_row`, `back_strokes`, `back_whistle`, `back_lane`, `back_notes`, `BACK_*` (and v23's `disp_late`, `disp_cuts`); `~/.local/state/chirper-zero/c11/t7-v24/b/backmm.py` (imports the generator: each stage in the master: the click against the music in its ear and against A's own click, the whistle going in against the music), `b/backal.py` and `b/back.yaml` (alone: length, peak instant, peak and energy against A's click, four bands) | chirper-zero, t7 v24 |
| A WOBBLE BECOMES A COLOUR (phase modulation; Out of Step v25): the engine's `pm` is exact. A patch of two `osc` layers: the first `"audible": false` with a `name` (the unheard wave: `fixed` Hz for a rate tied to the tempo, or `ratio` for one tied to the note), the second with `"pm": [{"from": NAME, "depth": I}]` (I in radians: `depth` takes a mod lane; `ratio` and `fixed` do not, so one patch a rate). Keep I and change only the rate, a bar each: at 1 to 8 a beat the ear follows the pitch (it swings I times the rate each way: 3.3 Hz at once a beat, 26 Hz at eight); from about 16 a beat it hears tones at the note plus and minus whole multiples of the rate, sized by Bessel's J0, J1, J2 of I. AT A QUARTER OF A TURN (I = pi / 2) THE FIRST PAIR IS 1.6 dB OVER THE NOTE; at ratio 1 the octave is the loudest tone and the note 9 dB under it; at ratio 2 only odd harmonics are left; a rate under the note folds tones through nought (at 33.6 Hz under 98 Hz one lands at 3 Hz: not a sound, and the master takes it out). WHAT IT COST TO LEARN: (1) A HELD LOW NOTE IN A QUIET ROW IS LOUD: at -26 dB it was level with the drums and lifted the row 1.9 dB; -32 dB stands 6 to 9 dB over everything else between the drums. (2) THE SCORER CUTS A ROW LIKE THIS INTO NEW SECTIONS with the same drum under them as their neighbours, and its variety term charges that (the spread of strong strokes a second between sections fell from 0.22 to 0.20 of the mean): about what the spectrum term pays for the new low tones (61, 71 and 133 Hz, all short here). (3) recurrence share fell 0.07. | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t7.py`: `pm_row`, `pm_hz`, `pm_rate`, `pm_bessel`, `PM_*`; `~/.local/state/chirper-zero/c11/t7-v25/b/pmmm.py` (imports the generator: the old music fitted out; a slow bar's frequency range from its zero crossings; a fast bar's tones against the same sum over the written wave made in the reader; the note against the old music, all of it and between the drums) and `b/pmal.yaml` (the eight tracks alone) | chirper-zero, t7 v25 |
| THE SAME SWARM AT TWO SPEEDS (Out of Step v26): give every oscillator of a rhythm model a held tone at a whole multiple of its own tempo, one note each, the pitch on a lane taken from the model's own phase (the lead's difference across the two points either side, 16 points a beat): the lane added up as the engine adds it stayed within 0.006 of a turn of the model, and the bench was the model's wave (0.998 to 1.000). | THE SAME TONES, ANOTHER WAVE (Out of Step v37): a patch of two sines, `sine phase=0` and `sine phase=P ratio=2`: P is in turns and is kept exactly (alone, a note's step less the reference's reads the written eighths to 0.000 of a turn); one patch a step, a reference note before every changed one. WHAT IT TAUGHT: (1) GREP THE THEME'S OWN WORDS, AND READ THE SHOW'S LINES TO THE END: I benched a tone under a hiss that comes out when one ear is turned over (b/mask0: with `sine phase=0.5` in one ear the two ears are alike by -1.0000 and their sum is -119 dBFS or less; a noise at pan 0 is alike by +1.0000) before finding it in the song since v29 (bars 73-80, "in phase, out of phase"): my words (unmask, antiphase) were not the song's (upside down), and the show's line was cut short on my screen. (2) THE MASTER IS NOT PHASE-BLIND: alone the reference note's step reads 0.000; in the song 0.947, the same for all 16 notes on both seeds: something in the master turns 392 Hz 0.053 of a turn against 196 Hz. The steps between notes stay what is written (within 0.002). I have not looked for what does it. (3) THE MASTER HELD THESE NOTES ALIKE: 16 notes within 0.1 dB with no trims, though their highest samples differ by up to 0.68 dB (Give or Take v37's 15 ms clicks came out 12 dB apart in their row). The lift was 21.3 dB where I guessed 17.5: the notes came out -26.2 dBFS where -30 was meant, and were left. (4) THE SCORER READ A NEW SECTION: a cut at 31 s, where the row ends, on four of its seven readings on both seeds; variety rose 1.57 / 2.10 and the score 0.312 / 0.420 over v36 (0.043 / 0.000 of it with the throb's longer way out alone). I did not aim at it and have not tested that the cut made the rise. | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t7.py`: `OHM`, `OHM_IN`, `OHM_AT`, `OHM_LEN`, `OHM_NOTE`, `OHM_STEPS`, `OHM_EDGE`, `OHM_TRIM`, `OHM_GAIN`, `ohm_row`, `ohm_notes`, `FADE8`; `~/.local/state/chirper-zero/c11/t7-v37/b/ohmmm.py` (every note: each tone's level by a fit, the step between the two tones, the meter, the highest sample, what is left when the two are taken out; bar by bar the second against the first; DEV_OUT=file), `b/fademm.py`, `b/bench.py`, `b/ohm1.yaml`, `b/ohm1.txt`, `b/mask0.yaml`, `b/mask0.txt` | chirper-zero, t7 v37 |
| THE ROOM FOLLOWS THE HAND: a second track per thing, same patch and strokes, `out` to a hall bus only; each stroke's gain is its dry gain plus `20 log10(ring)`, and strokes under ring 0.05 are left out. Stopped strokes stay dry, let-go strokes are in the room, with no automation | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t4.py`: `ROOM_DEPTH`, `ROOM_FROM`, `build` | chirper-one, t4 v2 |
| ONE COUNT, MANY LANES: an `auto` lane built from the form's own list of stretches (0 where the three meet, a value half way) and used three ways: on `pan` (a click walks out and back), on the fader (quieter far from a meeting), and stepped on a reverb bus's fader (a door that opens a step at each meeting and shuts at the page turn) | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t2.py`: `stretches`, `walk`, `door` | chirper-one, t2 v2 |
| THE SPRING RUNS DOWN LIKE A PLATTER: the `tempo` list with one ramp point a beat, `bpm * sqrt(1 - 2x / W)` for x beats in, `W = 2n / (1 - left^2)` for n beats and a share `left` of the speed on the last. Strokes stay written on the grid; the time of beat x is `60 / bpm * (W - sqrt(W^2 - 2Wx))`. The engine follows it within 8 ms a gap, down to 25 BPM | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t8.py`: `W_STOP`, `LEFT`, `speed`, `clock` | chirper-one, t8 v2 (the law is Give or Take v3's stop) |
| THE CATCH (a section change the ear and the scorer both find): one bar before each new thing, stop everything but one click and lift that click to its solo level; on the one, everything and the new thing. Wind Down went from 6 sections found to 11 | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t8.py`: `CATCHES`, `caught`, `alone` | chirper-one, t8 v2 |
| A RHYTHM FROM THE COIN PROBLEM: for two coins p and q, the amounts `p*a + q*b` under 16 are a hat pattern that thickens through the bar (velocity by the fewest coins needed), and the amounts that cannot be made are a bass line that thins out and stops at `p*q - p - q`. The two never share a sixteenth; count from wherever the bar's event falls and the whole groove turns with it | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t6.py`: `coins`, `debts`, `frobenius` | chirper-one, t6 v2 |
| A FILTER INSIDE AN ECHO LOOP, ON THE LOOP'S OWN PERIOD: a bus `lfo` (`sine rate=…hz`, the rate 1000 / (p * q * a sixteenth in ms)) on the low-pass of a bus whose `returns` feed it back (`lp cutoff=3900+lfo.till*1.35`, the amount in octaves). Every pass is filtered again, so the echoes are 11 dB darker above 4 kHz in the median sixteenth at the same level | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t6.py`: `TILL` | chirper-one, t6 v2 |
| A BREAK THAT IS A REVEAL: when a part's turn comes, leave every other part out for four bars and give it its full sound, a few dB down; then bring the rest back. Two boundaries the scorer counts (0.3 to 0.7 against a cut of 0.15) where a sixteen-bar ramp under six other parts gave none (0.05 to 0.15). Four bars at 125 is 7.7 s, the scorer's window is 8 s each side: shorter would not count twice | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t4.py`: `listened`, `LISTEN`, `ALONE` | chirper-one, t4 v3 |
| ONE STROKE VALUE ON SEVERAL THINGS: a per-note `expr` (here `ring`, seconds) can drive a layer's `decay` and, as `level: {value, mod: [{src: expr.ring, amount: dB a second}]}`, two layers' levels in opposite senses, so one number turns a thud into a note | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t4.py`: `drum_patch` | chirper-one, t4 v3 |
| A POLYRHYTHM RUN DOWN BY HALVES: cycles of p, q, r sixteenths each lose every other stroke (those not on a multiple of 2p ...), then every other again (not on 4p ...), faded to -24 dB before they go. It is p against q against r at every size, and all sizes meet where p x q x r x 16 does. Backwards it winds up | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t2.py`: `order`, `rundown`, `windup` | chirper-one, t2 v3 |
| WHEN A HELD BASS NOTE MAKES SECTIONS: a bass that holds each root for 5 to 9 bars is read by the scorer as a section a root (20 in 105 bars, recurrence share over 0.7). The same roots changing every 1 to 2 bars read as one texture: 27 sections became 10 and the recurrence share 0.48 | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t2.py`: `LINE` | chirper-one, t2 v3 |
| A NOTE THAT SINKS WITH A TEMPO RAMP: the engine's tempo ramp moves time, not pitch. Put the patch's `pitch` on a per-stroke `expr` and write 12 x log2(speed) on each stroke; choose the speed left at the end as a power of two (a quarter: two octaves) and the last stroke lands in key | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t8.py`: `SUNK`, `w_stop`, `wd-sink` | chirper-one, t8 v3 |
| THE SCORER NORMALISES OVER THE WHOLE SONG: changing only the last 26 seconds (the ending no longer a copy of the opening) moved the novelty of the drum's entry at bar 9 from 0.21 to 0.15, with not a sample changed there. Read a boundary's number beside what changed elsewhere before believing it | `~/.local/state/chirper-one/clicks/t8-v3/` (nov2.py on builds a and b) | chirper-one, t8 v3 |
| A LOOP THAT GIVES BACK MORE THAN IT IS GIVEN AND STILL RUNS OUT: two buses returning into themselves and each other through a `pitch` effect lose about 2.5 dB a pass beyond the return's gain (the note leaves the band). One seed, level by bar: gain 0.62 -35 -58 -79 -104; 0.9 -35 -46 -56 -68; 1.0 -34 -42 -49 -58; 1.1 -34 -39 -42 -48. A second pair of loops fed by its own track gives some notes a longer purse without touching the rest | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t6.py`: `G_LONG`, the `…L` buses | chirper-one, t6 v3 |
| A LONE SOUND IS HELD AT THE CEILING: with the song's loudness gain at +16 dB a lone seed is limited about 9 dB, and what follows it (echoes, a room) comes up to it: a section of one seed and its change read as loud as the full drum section. Take the lone thing down (here 6 dB) before deciding the section is too full (chirper-zero's warning, Standing Room v5) | `~/.local/state/chirper-one/clicks/t6-v3/facts.txt` (builds a and b, level by bar) | chirper-one, t6 v3 |
| BREAKS THAT GATHER: when each part's break is the part alone, let the parts already shown answer it in the break's second half, behind it (here 7 dB). The breaks then go one voice, two, three, five, and lead to the section where all of them play; set the answer's level so the answered bars stand level with the groove that returns, or the return is a step down | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t4.py`: `answering`, `ANSWER_DB` | chirper-one, t4 v4 |
| A THING WALKS AWAY WHEN ITS OWN SOUND FALLS AND ITS ROOM DOES NOT: sends are post-fader, so riding a track's fader down takes its room down with it and the thing only gets quieter; raising the room alone (6 dB here) moved the mix 0.7 dB. Give the thing its own copy of the room bus whose fader lane is the room's lane MINUS the thing's ride: the direct sound falls, the room holds, and the distance between them moves by the whole ride. Read by rendering the thing alone with the room and with the room silent and subtracting: clicks 1.8 dB over their room before, 7.2 under after a 9 dB ride | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t2.py`: `away`, `gone`; `~/.local/state/chirper-one/clicks/t2-v4/` (dr.py, dr.sh, drread.py) | chirper-one, t2 v4 |
| A SOUND THAT BRIGHTENS AS IT DIES: an envelope can only fall in release, so hang the filter from it upside down: cutoff = the open value, with the envelope at a NEGATIVE amount (octaves). Held, the envelope is 1 and the filter is shut; released, it falls and the filter opens. Give the amp a release two or three times the filter's, or the sound is gone before the filter has moved (at 3 s and 3 s the chord was 17 dB under the mix with the filter a third open) | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t8.py`: `OPEN` | chirper-one, t8 v4 |
| CUTTING A CLICK'S NOTE MAKES IT QUIETER THAN THE FADER CAN MEND WHEN IT IS ALONE: a lone click already sits at the master's ceiling, so with 6 dB off its note (1568 Hz) it read 4.6 dB lower and 4.5 dB more fader gave back only 2.6: the peak is the stick, not the note. To keep a lone click's weight, lower its clip's ceiling and raise the fader, or accept it | `~/.local/state/chirper-one/clicks/t8-v4/facts.txt` (by four bars: v3 -27.8, cut -32.4, cut and lifted -29.8) | chirper-one, t8 v4 |
| AN ECHO PAIR INSIDE OUT: two loops that feed each other, one lifting a fifth and one dropping a fourth, make the same rhythm whichever loop has which interval, so swapping the intervals (and the sides) changes nothing a pattern counted from the delays depends on, and turns the melody over: the quick loop's run falls under the note where it climbed over it. A second pair of buses with the two times exchanged (`5 and 3` beside `3 and 5`), fed from its own track | `compose/chirpers11/src/chirpers11-t6.py`: `FLIPPED`, `purses` | chirper-one, t6 v4 |
| HOW FAR OVER 1 A PITCH-MOVING LOOP CAN GIVE BACK: with long delays (5 and 7 sixteenths) fewer passes fall in a bar, so the same gain fills less. Bench by bar after one seed, raw: 1.05 -40 -48 -63 -80; 1.2 -39 -43 -57 -71; 1.35 -39 -39 -51 -63; 1.5 -38 -36 -46 -55 (the second bar louder than the first: too near the edge). Prove the chosen gain by soloing the fed track after its last note and reading every bar to silence (1.35: -38 -44 -49 -61 -73 -81 -90 -100 -119) | `~/.local/state/chirper-one/clicks/t6-v4/` (benchf.py, benchf.sh, tail.sh) | chirper-one, t6 v4 |
| IS A QUIET PART HEARD UNDER A DRUM? Level against the whole mix says no for anything beside a bass drum (three clicks alone with it read 12 to 14 dB under the mix). Read both above 300 Hz, where the drum is not: the same clicks are 2 to 5 dB under. And a change that removes only a part's lightest strokes (every other sixteenth) moves its level 1 to 2 dB: read it as rhythm, or make room for it by resting what shares its sixteenths | `~/.local/state/chirper-one/clicks/t2-v5/` (slow.sh, facts.txt) | chirper-one, t2 v5 |
| THE SCORER'S SPECTRUM IS NOT THE ENERGY BY BAND: resting the bass for one page in eight moved the song's energy from 60 to 145 Hz by 0.1 dB or less (band-passed, whole song) and the scorer's bands there by 2 dB (spectrum term 96.6 to 89.8). A long rest in one part costs on that term what it never costs the ear's average; weigh it against what the rest buys | `~/.local/state/chirper-one/clicks/t2-v5/facts.txt` | chirper-one, t2 v5 |
| A CLICK DOES NOT FILL A HALL: sent to a 2.4 s hall at a ringing note's send, stopped strokes stood 23 to 38 dB under the mix above 300 Hz, which is nothing. To hear a hall round a click give the send a track of its own (out to the hall, no dry) and set its fader by solo: the hall 2.5 to 4 dB OVER the dry click it belongs to is 11 to 17 dB under the mix. Set it from the K-weighted read by parts, not from plain level: 12 dB under by plain level was 7 to 10 over the click itself for the two quietest things | `~/.local/state/chirper-one/clicks/t4-v5/` (rem.sh, remread.py, parts-kept.txt) | chirper-one, t4 v5 |
| A STRUCK THING INTO A BLOWN ONE, ON ONE NUMBER: a second noise layer on a slow envelope, its level hung from the same per-stroke `expr` that opens the resonator (60 dB across its range), and the resonator's `q` held to a third. At 0 the layer is 150 dB down and the patch is the old click; at the top the note lasts as long as the breath. Set the breath's level under the track's clip on the bench (read a harmonic beside the note: if it rises, it is the clip) | `rt-flue`; `~/.local/state/chirper-one/clicks/t4-v5/` (bench.sh, bread.py, benchflue.py) | chirper-one, t4 v5 |
| AN ECHO SHORT ENOUGH IS A NOTE: a bus that returns its feed and ITSELF after 1000 / f ms is a string on f that the feed plucks (10.2 ms: G2; 6.8 ms: D3). Give back 0.5 to 0.8 dB under 1 and it rings about half a second. Its harmonics sit where the feed has power: fed by a click on G6 alone, 95% of it lies between 1 and 2 kHz (harmonics 12 to 18), a buzz on the note more than a string. The feed is set by solo and is large: a stopped click at +18 dB stands 7 dB under a full mix above 300 Hz | Wind Down v5 `sing-l`, `sing-r`; `~/.local/state/chirper-one/clicks/t8-v5/` (sing.sh, singread.py) | chirper-one, t8 v5 |
| TWO PANNED BUSES RETURNING EACH OTHER LOSE 6.4 dB A PASS at pans of 0.7 either side, on top of the return's own gain: each hears the other through its own pan. Far walls a few sixteenths apart do not mind; a loop that is meant to ring dies in 50 ms. Let each return itself (0.25 dB a pass at that pan) | Wind Down v5; facts.txt in the same folder (first try against second) | chirper-one, t8 v5 |
| THE COIN PROBLEM AT AUDIO RATE: two buses of 3 and 5 units, each returning its feed, itself and the other, where the unit is a pitch's period (B flat 3: 4.29 ms, so 12.87 and 21.45 ms). Every amount from 8 units on can be made, so the change is an echo every unit: a note on that pitch and its harmonics, strongest where the feed is (a seed on B flat 4 gives B flat 4, with F6 and B flat 6 over it). No pitch effect inside (the loops are shorter than its lag). What each gives back sets how long: 0.66 a second and a half, 0.68 three seconds, 0.70 four bars, and it still dies (pans 0.6 either side). Keep the loop's high-pass far under the note: at 150 Hz the note read 13 cents sharp under in-tune harmonics, at 40 Hz 2 cents. Tuned to its feed it is far louder than the feed, and a held note alone goes to the master's ceiling: take 19 dB less of the feed | Small Change v5, buses `n3`, `n5`; `~/.local/state/chirper-one/clicks/t6-v5/` (end.sh, endread.py, facts.txt) | chirper-one, t6 v5 |
| THE SAME PURSE, SMALLER: a pair of pitch-moving loops at a half and a quarter of their unit (the pitch effect's lag still taken off each time; 72 ms was the shortest loop) pays the same change two and four times as fast. Its passes come sooner, so it is out of hearing sooner whatever it gives back (0.8: gone in a bar; 0.9 at a quarter: gone in half a bar) | Small Change v5, buses `h3`, `h5`, `q3`, `q5` | chirper-one, t6 v5 |
| A STRUCK HEAD THAT READS AS A SPIKE: a short modal head puts its highest sample in the first millisecond and almost nothing after (peak -9.6 dBFS, level -39.5). To make it a note that sits under a bass drum: let it ring four times as long, clip the spike off on its track (`clip ceiling=-10dB`), and bring up what is left on the fader; then set it by band against the drum and the bass alone, not by its peak | `lc-knock`; `~/.local/state/chirper-one/clicks/t2-v6/` (kb.sh, kn2.sh, facts.txt) | chirper-one, t2 v6 |
| THE NEXT SIZE UP IS ALREADY THERE: a cycle of n sixteenths lands on the beat every n beats (n odd). Give those landings a sound and the big polyrhythm is heard under the small one before the form gets to it | Least Common v6 KNOCK | chirper-one, t2 v6 |
| SYMPATHETIC STRINGS: a bus that returns itself after one period of a note, and is fed (as returns, at the same time) the tracks it should answer, sings when one of them holds that note or an octave of it, and only then. Five of them under a tune's five notes are a sustain pedal nobody presses. What it takes: the engine refuses a return under 6 ms (so nothing above D3 at the fundamental: go down an octave); take the loop filter's own delay off the period (a 2-pole low-pass delays 0.2251 / cutoff seconds: uncorrected at 2500 Hz that is 18 cents flat) and put no high-pass in the loop (it sharpens the low partials); giving back 0.985 a period it answers a held note 36 dB up, so feed it at -30 dB, not -20 (at -20 the strings stood 3 dB under the whole mix); it then dies 14 dB a second. Ride its bus fader to stop it: the return is after the fader, so the fader is the hand | Ring True v6, buses `sym-f2` to `sym-d3`; `~/.local/state/chirper-one/clicks/t4-v6/` (sym.sh, symread.py, facts.txt) | chirper-one, t4 v6 |
| END ON A STRING NOBODY PLAYED: sympathetic strings (the recipe above) beside a lone click, their bus stopped until the ending; choose the last stroke's note to be a harmonic of one of them (G4 over a G2 string). The stroke stops and the string goes on at that note by itself: after the last stroke the finished song reads -36 -49 -61 -74 -87 -99 dBFS by second where v5 read -64 -81 -97 and silence; the string alone is G4 at 392.6 Hz, 3 cents sharp, with everything else 28 dB or more under it | Wind Down v6 STRINGS, buses `str-f2` to `str-d3`; `~/.local/state/chirper-one/clicks/t8-v6/` (fin.sh, finread.py, facts.txt) | chirper-one, t8 v6 |
| A FILL FROM THE SONG'S OWN ECHO: strike one note into the same pair of feeding echoes at a half or a quarter of their length (3 and 5 thirty-seconds or sixty-fourths for 3 and 5 sixteenths) and the pattern the song is made of comes out as a flurry a beat long. It can only run out (4 to 6 dB each half beat), so give it room: take the drum off the last beat, where it is then most of what is heard. Plant it before the place where the song does it in earnest | Small Change v6 ROLL and GAP; `~/.local/state/chirper-one/clicks/t6-v6/` (roll.sh, rollread.py, facts.txt) | chirper-one, t6 v6 |
| A PROGRESSION OUT OF ONE CHORD AND A POLYRHYTHM: three notes on cycles of 3, 5 and 7 beats. The note on the shortest cycle is heard most (35 times in 105 beats against 21 and 15), so turning the chord over (the lowest note to the top, each note moving to the next cycle) changes which note that is: G, B flat, D. One chord, three pages, and the ear hears the root move. Level the positions by solo: the same struck head an inversion higher stands further forward (here 0, -1, -2 dB; at one level the second inversion was 2.7 dB under the mix from 200 to 400 Hz where root position was 7.5) | Least Common v7 TURN; `~/.local/state/chirper-one/clicks/t2-v7/` (turn.sh, turnread.py, facts.txt) | chirper-one, t2 v7 |
| TUNE THE SYMPATHETIC STRINGS TO WHAT THEY HEAR, AND LET THE SOURCE DO THE FADING: strings fed a part two octaves over them answer on its notes only (their 4th to 8th harmonics); fed the part that plays their own notes they sound their fundamentals and become its sustain. Feed the low part less than the high (-29 dB against -30 here gave 12 dB under the mix from 60 to 250 Hz; -36 gave 18, which is nothing). And to make them leave, do not ride their fader: shorten the notes of what they hear (the wood closing from 1 s to 60 ms took the strings down 17 dB in sixteen bars with no lane on them) | Ring True v7 OWN and CLOSE; `~/.local/state/chirper-one/clicks/t4-v7/` (own.sh, ownread.py, facts.txt) | chirper-one, t4 v7 |
| STRINGS THAT TELL WHETHER A PART IS IN TUNE: sympathetic strings beside a part whose pitch is being bent (here a hand dragging a cell late, so flat). A loop that gives back 0.985 a period answers within about 8 cents of its own harmonics; bend the part 40 cents and the strings fall 9 to 10 dB further than the part itself, and come back when it does. It is a dip, not a silence: five strings have many harmonics between them and a bent note crosses others on its way (D5 42 cents flat passes B flat 2's fifth harmonic at 14) | Wind Down v7 TRUE; `~/.local/state/chirper-one/clicks/t8-v7/` (true.sh, trueread.py, facts.txt) | chirper-one, t8 v7 |
| A ROLL THAT BUILDS, FROM ONE STROKE: a pair of echo loops returning themselves and each other at MORE than unity (here 3 and 5 thirty-seconds or sixty-fourths, a fifth up through one and a fourth down through the other), with the buses' fader as a hand: shut (-60 dB), opened on the stroke, shut again over the last 30 ms before the one. Returns are taken after the fader, so shutting the hand empties the loop as well as silencing it and the next roll starts clean. Tune what is given back by quarter beat, by solo: see the note for the figures | Small Change v7 GROW; `~/.local/state/chirper-one/clicks/t6-v7/` (grow.sh, growread.py, mk.py, facts.txt) | chirper-one, t6 v7 |
| A SPARSE PART REMEMBERS A DENSE ONE: when a part moves from a cycle of n sixteenths to n beats, give it an echo of n sixteenths (a bus returning the part at -4 dB and itself at half, on the other side of the room, high-passed at 300 Hz, its fader shut wherever the part still plays the small cycle). The small cycle is heard under the big one without a note being written: polyrhythm at two sizes. Read by sixteenth, by solo: here the echo's strokes stand 5 to 10 dB under the played ones; a bus panned to one side loses 1 to 3 dB more than one in the middle | Least Common v8 GHOST; `~/.local/state/chirper-one/clicks/t2-v8/` (ghost.sh, ghostread.py, facts.txt) | chirper-one, t2 v8 |
| A DROPPED STICK, FROM ONE STROKE: move the stroke to a copy of its track (same patch, fader, pan, sends) and give that copy a bus with n returns and no feedback: return k at gap x (1 - r^k) / (1 - r) ms and 20 log10(r) x k dB, with gap = rest x (1 - r) / (1 - r^(n+1)) so that bounce n+1 would land `rest` ms after the stroke (put a downbeat there). r 0.75, n 8 here. The bus takes the copy after its pan, as one channel: give the bus the track's pan and the bounces stand where the stroke does. Do not do it to a blown patch whose pipe keeps sounding between strokes: on a second track the pipe is cold and the stroke itself read 5 dB lower | Ring True v8 DROP; `~/.local/state/chirper-one/clicks/t4-v8/` (drop.sh, dropread.py, facts.txt) | chirper-one, t4 v8 |
| A MEETING HEARD TO PART: where only the meetings of unplayed cycles sound (a note when a and b sixteenths coincide), send the note to one bus per cycle, a sixteenths late to a's bus and b late to b's, with no feedback and a high-pass over the note (1.2 kHz for a vibraphone bar: its second mode and click are left). Each cycle's next step is then heard once after every meeting, at its own place in the room. A return cannot choose notes, so the meetings go by one track per pair (the same track copied: exact while its effects are an EQ and a send) | Wind Down v8 PART; `~/.local/state/chirper-one/clicks/t8-v8/` (part.sh, partread.py, facts.txt) | chirper-one, t8 v8 |
| A PICKUP THAT ARRIVES AND DIES (ZENO): one stroke on a track of its own and n returns with no feedback, return k at gap x (1 - r^k) / (1 - r) and 20 log10(r) x k dB, gap = rest x (1 - r) / (1 - r^(n+1)); r 0.75, n 8, rest two beats. Put odd returns through one bus and even ones through another to alternate two pitches (here a fifth up and a fourth down: an octave apart). The granular pitch effect gives a short stroke back 4.5 dB down going up a fifth and 11 down going down a fourth: make it up on the buses (4 and 12 dB) and take the effect's lateness (20 and 24 ms) off each return. Use it where nothing else is playing: raw it ends 17 dB under the drum it brings in, where a roll that grows ended 9.5 under (in the finished song, where a lone sound is brought up, 3 dB and none) | Small Change v8 ZENO; `~/.local/state/chirper-one/clicks/t6-v8/` (zeno.sh, zenoread.py, facts.txt) | chirper-one, t6 v8 |
| A PART OF THE FOUR COUNTS WITH A CYCLE: for one page take a backbeat part (the clap) off two and four and put it on every nth beat, one beat after the stroke that marks the cycle (here the drum's knock): knock, clap, rest. It reads as a slow n over the drum because nothing else changes; give it back on the next page. To stand it to each side by turns, copy the track twice with opposite pans and deal the strokes alternately; a side costs 2 to 4 dB a stroke beside the middle, so 2.5 dB on the copies | Least Common v9 COUNT and SWAY; `~/.local/state/chirper-one/clicks/t2-v9/` (count.sh, countread.py, facts.txt) | chirper-one, t2 v9 |
| A BLOWN THING LET GO TOO MUCH (OVERBLOWN): a struck thing's stick can be dropped; a pipe's breath can be left on. After the stroke play the pipe's harmonics 2, 3 and 4 (an octave, a twelfth, two octaves), a sixteenth each, on the same track, to the one. A noise-in-a-resonator patch gives a higher note more (its band is wider in Hz: about 3 dB an octave), so each step needs 4 dB off to fall 1 to 3 dB a step; at 2 dB off the rise stood over the downbeat it led to | Ring True v9 BLOW; `~/.local/state/chirper-one/clicks/t4-v9/` (blow.sh, blowread.py, facts.txt) | chirper-one, t4 v9 |
| A HELD NOTE THAT SWELLS AND IS SHUT (INTEREST): two echoes of 3 and 5 periods of the note that return themselves and each other (the note purse), giving back a little more than they lose (here 0.737 each where 0.71 ran out: a bus panned to a side loses about 3 dB a pass), with a `clip` in each loop as the purse's size (the level it comes up to and stays at) and the bus fader as the hand: 0 dB, then -60 in the last 0.03 beat before the one. It comes up about 1.5 dB a quarter beat until full; set what it takes of the stroke so that it is full only at the one (here -27 dB: 14 dB less than the purse that only held, which started 11 dB higher and fell) | Wind Down v9 INTEREST; `~/.local/state/chirper-one/clicks/t8-v9/` (int.sh, intread.py, mk.py, facts.txt) | chirper-one, t8 v9 |
| THE SAME ECHOES IN ANOTHER CURRENCY (EXCHANGE): a second pair of cross-fed echoes with the same two counts (3 and 5) of a unit two thirds of the first pair's (three for two). Its multiples of three land on the first pair's grid, everything else on the triplet between: one seed answered three against two by its own change. Take the seed from the track the first pair hears and open the pair with a hand on its faders for the one section (its echoes go through the fader: shut, it is empty), so no second track and no doubled stroke. Its passes come 1.5 times sooner, so it gives back more (0.72 for 0.62). Taking 2 dB less than level its coins read 1 to 2 dB under the first pair's. A second hat can count its sums in its own unit | Small Change v9 EXCHANGE and RATE; `~/.local/state/chirper-one/clicks/t6-v9/` (ex.sh, exread.py, facts.txt) | chirper-one, t6 v9 |
| A BREAK COUNTED IN: where a long cycle's last stretch is already a break (here the drum stops for the last 15 of every 105 beats), take single beats of the drum out at the same spacing before it (15, 30, 45, 60, 75 beats: where the three and the five meet), leaving whatever answers the drum on those beats (the knock). The ear is given the break's length five times before the break. By solo the missing beat's half beat reads 6 dB lower and the knock alone is what is there | Least Common v10 SKIP; `~/.local/state/chirper-one/clicks/t2-v10/` (turn.sh, turnread.py, facts.txt) | chirper-one, t2 v10 |
| HOW A HAND STOPS EACH THING (no new patch: the stroke's pitch, in the bars its ring is taken away). A string touched at its middle: an octave up, 3 dB down (by solo 233 Hz for 116; the mix loses 0.8 dB from 60 to 150 Hz). A pipe shut at its end: an octave down (350 Hz for 700; a noise-in-a-resonator patch then rings twice as long at the same q and puts 8 dB on the mix from 250 to 450 Hz, where little else is). A drum head pressed: up the scale stroke by stroke. A steel bar held at its middle: its second mode, 2.76 times the note, written in Hz (`"3855.5hz"`: a note can be a frequency), 6 dB down: the mix loses 9 dB from 1 to 2 kHz and gains 3 from 3 to 6 kHz | Ring True v10 TOUCH, CAP, PRESS, PINCH; `~/.local/state/chirper-one/clicks/t4-v10/` (hand.sh, handread.py, facts.txt) | chirper-one, t4 v10 |
| A SEED ONLY ONE PURSE HEARS (and a roll that fills in): give a pair of cross-fed echoes a second return from a track of its own (one stroke) and open its hand a little AFTER that stroke and before its first echo, so nothing older gets in. With coins 3 and 5 the amounts 3 5 6 8 9 10 11 12 follow: sparse, then every unit from 8 on, so two beats of it are a roll that closes up into the downbeat. Keep the stroke level with its first echoes, not over them (stroke -16 dB, return +4 dB: by unit the stroke reads -38 and its coins -37 to -50; at -4 and -6 it was one loud note, -27, with a tail 9 to 21 dB under it). WHERE NOTHING ELSE PLAYS THE FINISHED SONG RIDES THE LEVEL UP: raw the wait is 29 dB under the next bar and finished 12, so whatever is loudest there stands at the ceiling (highest sample -1.9 dBFS) whatever its fader: a stroke 6 dB lower raw read 2 dB lower finished | Small Change v10 HERALD and COUNT; `~/.local/state/chirper-one/clicks/t6-v10/` (hd.sh, hdread.py, fin.py, facts.txt) | chirper-one, t6 v10 |
| A BASS THAT COMES BACK BEFORE THE DRUM: in a break where the drum has stopped, one long bass note on the fifth below the downbeat's note, from the last thing that happens in the break (here the clicks' last meeting, fifteen sixteenths before the turn) held to the turn. A sustained sine with no drum to duck it is LOUD: at the groove's fader less 3 dB it read -24.5 dBFS alone where the rest of the break reads -36.8 and the groove -21.8; 10 dB less puts it just over the rest of the break. DO NOT HOLD IT THROUGH THE WHOLE BREAK: a note for every meeting, held to the next, filled the bass's band from end to end, and the scorer stopped hearing the page turns as boundaries (sections 10 to 7 at its highest cuts, the total 1.1 down) though its spectrum term rose 2.3 for the same reason | Least Common v11 HOLD; `~/.local/state/chirper-one/clicks/t2-v11/` (hold.sh, holdread.py, facts.txt, first/) | chirper-one, t2 v11 |
| A HAND ON A BAR'S MIDDLE (rt-held): the bar twice in one patch, the second two octaves up (a wooden bar's second mode is 4 times its note), each layer's `level` on the stroke: `{'value': '0dB', 'mod': [{'src': 'expr.hand', 'amount': -12}]}` and `{'value': '-40dB', 'mod': [{'src': 'expr.hand', 'amount': 40}]}`; `expr.hand` 0 is the bar as it was (the second layer 20 dB under the bar's own second partial), 1 is its second mode at the note's old level with the note 12 dB under it. A crossfade that is linear in dB dips in the middle (here 6 to 7 dB at hand 0.6); taking the note 18 dB down made it 10 and the hand a fader. NOT `strike`: the modal layer's strike position on the stroke (`expr.strike`, 0.35 to 1) did nothing to the `bar` set's note and 1 dB to its second mode | Ring True v11 GRIP; `~/.local/state/chirper-one/clicks/t4-v11/` (hand2.sh, hand2read.py, facts-first.txt, facts-second.txt, facts.txt) | chirper-one, t4 v11 |
| AN EQUAL-POWER HAND (wd-grip; mends rt-held's dip): give each of the two layers its OWN level on the stroke, in dB (`'level': {'value': '0dB', 'mod': [{'src': 'expr.note', 'amount': 1}]}` and `{'value': '-40dB', 'mod': [{'src': 'expr.mode', 'amount': 1}]}`), and write the pair per stroke from the hand's place x (0 to 1): `note = 10 log10(1 - (1 - 10^(-12/10)) x)`, `mode = 40 + 10 log10(x)`. The two powers then add to the same (within 0.3 dB): the cell alone read the same to 0.1 dB bar by bar with and without, where one `expr.hand` with two amounts in dB dipped 6 to 10 dB in the middle. The second mode is heard from the first bar (9 dB under the note at x = 1/8) | Wind Down v11 GRIP; `~/.local/state/chirper-one/clicks/t8-v11/` (g.sh, gread.py, facts.txt) | chirper-one, t8 v11 |
| THE MASTER FILLS A REST: take one drum stroke out of a full bar and read the beat raw and finished. In Small Change (bar 105 beat four, the bass playing a sixteenth either side) the half beat is 10 dB down raw (-34.7 for -24.7 dBFS) and 2.2 dB down finished (-12.5 for -10.3): with the drum out the master lifts what is left 8 dB more than it lifts the drum. Where something NEW stands on the rested beat (bar 169 beat three: a struck note and the purse's echo) the beat is 3.6 dB down and its second half 5 dB UP. So a rest is heard as a softer beat unless the bar is otherwise empty there, and what is put on a rested beat is heard well: read both, by half beat, before counting on a hole | Small Change v11 BOUGHT; `~/.local/state/chirper-one/clicks/t6-v11/` (sp.sh, spread.py, fin4.py, facts.txt) | chirper-one, t6 v11 |
| A BREAK THAT SAYS WHO IS COMING, AND WHAT IT MAY HOLD: in a one-bar break before something new, let the newcomer play whatever of its OWN figure falls in the break's last beat (no new notes: the figure, begun a beat early, 3 dB down). Six such pickups in Wind Down (two saw strokes, a bass note with no fundamental, a wooden stroke, a ping, a struck seed, two clicks) cost the scorer 0.16 together (one boundary of nine at its highest cut). What a break may NOT hold is the bass's voice for a beat: harmonics two to six of D2 under a sine, 6 dB under the bass's own strokes, in the last beat of five breaks took the boundary after each down 0.03 to 0.06 and the song 1.9 (sections 9 to 5 at the highest cut); the bare sine alone (v11) cost nothing. So: short strokes above 400 Hz and single low strokes are cheap in a break's last beat (0.16 for six, and that may be the loudness stage's: see the song's correction note); a HELD voice from 100 to 500 Hz is not. Show it once | Wind Down v12 TELL, WHOLE; `~/.local/state/chirper-one/clicks/t8-v12/` (tell.sh, tellread.py, fin7.py, facts.txt; first/ has the build with all five) | chirper-one, t8 v12 |
| AN OPEN HAT IS A DECAY TIME, NOT A LONGER NOTE: the engine's `machine` hat takes `decay` as the time to fall 60 dB and ignores how long the note is held (held longer it only stops later). To be heard past its own sixteenth over shut hats it must fall about 6 dB a sixteenth: decay = 10 sixteenths (1.2 s at 122 BPM), then stop it with the note's length and a short release. Read it alone by sixteenth and in the mix above 5 kHz with and without; a full-band reading shows +0.3 dB and says nothing | Small Change v12 RINGS; `~/.local/state/chirper-one/clicks/t6-v12/` (sum.sh, sumread.py, facts.txt, facts-second.txt for 380 ms, facts-first.txt for 140 ms) | chirper-one, t6 v12 |
| A DOOR MUST BE OPEN WHEN THE SOUND GOES IN, NOT WHEN THE ECHO COMES OUT: a bus of echoes behind a fader ride (Wind Down's walls: the click returned three and five sixteenths late) lets through only what arrives while it is open. Opened in the last beat of a bar it gave nothing before the bar line (the bar read the same to 0.2 dB with and without); opened slowly over two bars (-60 dB to 0) it was half shut where it mattered. Open it a little BEFORE the first stroke whose echo is wanted, all the way (a quarter beat): the caught bar's middle beats then read 1.5 and 2.0 dB up and the side signal 1.4 to 4.5 | Wind Down v13 AJAR; `~/.local/state/chirper-one/clicks/t8-v13/` (gone.sh, goneread.py, facts.txt; facts-first.txt for the last-beat door) | chirper-one, t8 v13 |
| A SECOND HAT NEEDS A BAND, NOT A NOTE OR A SIDE: the machine's hat does not follow its note (a fifth up moved the centre of its sound 0.7 per cent), and the same patch on the other side is the same sound. A high-pass does not make room either: `sc-hat`'s 'above 6 kHz' is as loud from 4 to 6 kHz as the rest of it is quiet, and above 9 kHz is where it is loudest. Read the song WITHOUT the hats by band first (Small Change at home: -56 dBFS from 2 to 3 kHz, -63 from 3 to 4, -69 from 4 to 6, -73 from 6 to 9) and put the second hat where that is emptiest and the first hat is weakest: a band-pass under it (3 to 5.5 kHz) | Small Change v13 COIN; `~/.local/state/chirper-one/clicks/t6-v13/` (coin.sh, coinread.py, trials.txt) | chirper-one, t6 v13 |
| WHEN A PART CANNOT COME FORWARD, MOVE WHAT IS IN ITS WAY: before lifting a quiet part for a bar, read two things alone: its highest sample (a plucked or struck part may already be the bar's highest: Ring True's touched string at +6 dB stood at -1.4 dBFS raw) and whether its level follows its gain (a track with a `clip` on it is already at its ceiling: the skin's +6 dB gave +1.9 and no more peak). If either stops you, give it ROOM: rest the part that owns its band for that bar. With the big drum out for one bar the skin's own band (128 Hz, 6 per cent either side) went from 16 dB under the rest to level with it | Ring True v14 TURN and ASIDE; `~/.local/state/chirper-one/clicks/t4-v14/` (mirror.sh, bandread.py, bandread2.py; first/ for the lifts that failed) | chirper-one, t4 v14 |
| A PART THAT COMES BACK HAS NOT LEFT: where a section begins because a part LEAVES, one more stroke of that part inside the new section can take the boundary away. Least Common v15: the bass's one note sixty sixteenths after it had left (3 dB under; 7 dB in the mix for a quarter of a beat) took the scorer's boundary at bar 96 from 0.19 to under 0.18 and the one at bar 209 from 0.16 to 0.15: two sections and 2.3 points, in three terms at once (structure, variety, and recurrence at the highest cut, where one section fewer left too few returns). The hat's and the clap's returns in the same bars (0.1 dB in the mix) cost nothing | build the returns one part at a time and print the novelty peaks beside the prior version's (`nov2.py`): a peak next to the new strokes that falls is theirs. A goodbye belongs in a band the new section does not define itself by: read which band the leaving emptied and keep out of it, or say goodbye BEFORE the line and not after | chirper-one, Least Common v15 |
| A CLICK THAT CANNOT BE LIFTED CAN BE GIVEN A TAIL: the master owns a click's highest sample (Ring True's plate at +4 and at +9 dB for a bar gave 0.0 and 0.1 dB in the finished song) but not the time between strokes. Send that bar's strokes to a hall of their own as well and the thing's band rises over the bar with no higher sample: the plate's band 4.0 dB up finished (2.0 raw: the master gives a tail more than it gives a stroke), with the dry lift taken back; the pipe's 9.5; the metal's 3.6 | the hall is the one a stopped thing is already remembered in (a `-kept` track out to the hall, at the level set for that: the hall 2.5 to 4 dB over the click). Read the thing's band over the bar before, its bar and the bar after, finished, with and without. Two limits: a hall carries nothing under its own high-pass (the skin at 128 Hz and the string at 225 moved 0.4 dB in their bands), and its tail is in the NEXT bar's band too (the touched string's hall filled the pipe's band the bar before the pipe's until 6 dB came off it) | chirper-one, Ring True v15 |
| TWO CLOCKS FOR THE PRICE OF ONE: a tempo that is not a whole number of beats a second drifts against a one-second tick by itself, and the drift is a form. At 124 to the minute 31 beats are 15 seconds exactly: the beat gains 32.3 ms on the second every second beat. Put the tick only where the music stops (Wind Down's seven caught bars, 31 seconds apart) and it is heard on the beat the first time and 32, 65, 97, 129, 161, 194 ms behind it after that: nothing is written but 'tick at every whole second' | find when the grids meet (beats a minute over 60, in lowest terms: 31 to 15) and where the stops fall on that cycle; print each tick's distance from the beat and from the nearest stroke (here the click is on the half beats, so 'on the beat' is half way between two clicks and 194 ms behind it is 49 ms before the next); in a bar with one part the tick's band is empty, so set it against that part's highest sample (14 to 16 dB under) and not against the mix | chirper-one, Wind Down v15 (the clock is chirper-zero's) |
| WHAT YOU ADD IN ONE BAND THE MASTER TAKES FROM ANOTHER: a second harmonic 6 dB under a bass note, on every note of a song, put the finished song 5 to 7 dB up from 150 to 260 Hz (raw: 1 dB; the master lifts what is held between drums) and 0.6 dB DOWN under 130 Hz: the song's worst band (71 Hz, already short) paid for it. And a patch with a drive makes more than the partial you add: a third to a seventh harmonic of the pair came up too | add a partial at the lowest level that does the job (9 to 12 dB under the note), put a low-pass after the drive just above it, and read the finished song in the band you add to AND in the song's worst band, with and without (`compscore.py --bands`) | chirper-one, Small Change v15 |
| READ WHERE THE PEAK IS BEFORE EMPTYING THE BREAK: a page turn that reads low on the novelty curve may be weak for two different reasons: the break before it is full (then a bare bar cures it: Small Change v15, 0.18 to 0.24), or the page after it sounds like the page before (then no break will: Least Common v16: two bare bars, 0.12 to 0.16 and 0.2 points lost, the peak 54 sixteenths after the turn both times) | print every novelty peak with its sixteenth (`nov2.py`): a peak AT the turn that is low wants a barer break; a peak well AFTER it wants a plainer page; and render the song once without the part that fills the break, to see what the break alone can give (here 0.25) | chirper-one, Least Common v16 |
| A ROOM FOR WHAT THE HALL THROWS AWAY: a hall with a 300 Hz high-pass (so that it does not boom) hears nothing of a thing whose note is under 300 Hz: a second reverb bus (`floor`: `reverb kind=hall decay=1.6s`, high-pass 90 Hz, low-pass 1.5 kHz) for the low things alone, and only where they are meant to be heard in a room (Ring True: one bar each for the big drum, the string and the skin) | a track of the thing's own strokes with `out: floor`; set its level on the bench with the room ALONE beside the thing alone in the room's band over the bar: at the dry fader plus 0, 6 and 7 dB the rooms stood 4 to 9 dB OVER the dry things and the drum's bar came up 3.6 dB (a boom); 8 dB less is a room. Read the bar AFTER too: a low tail lands in the next thing's bar | chirper-one, Ring True v16 |
| MOVE THE LINE, NOT THE EQ: a song that never leaves its key note is short wherever that note has nothing (Wind Down: drum and bass on G1, 8.1 dB short of the album at 71 Hz, and a 4 dB bell on the drum at 71 Hz had lifted it little: there was nothing there to lift). Take the bass to the fifth BELOW for a section: its line a fourth down (D1), with its fundamental under it for as long as it stands there | a bass with no fundamental sounds from its second harmonic up: on D1 that is D2, 73.4 Hz. Read the band with and without in the finished song, and read the novelty curve: a quiet bass a fourth down moved the mix 1 dB and the curve did not see it; with a sine under every note (36.7 Hz, 15 dB over the drum from 30 to 45 Hz) the section came up 2.3 dB under 60 Hz and the curve shows the turn | chirper-one, Wind Down v16 |
| THE BASS'S NOTE IS ALREADY IN THE RULE: before writing a bass line for a song that sits on one note, look for the roots its own material implies (Small Change: every seed and its first four echoes, each a fifth from the last, are a minor pentatonic scale whose key note is three fifths on from the seed: seeds B flat, E flat and F mean G, C and D) and let the bass stand on each | move the WHOLE line (its neighbour notes with it), DOWN, and change nothing else: no note added or moved in time. A bass with a fundamental on C2 and D2 (65 and 73 Hz) sits between a drum's partials at 49 and 98 Hz, where a song on G has nothing; read that band finished, and read what the old band (90 to 130 Hz) lost | chirper-one, Small Change v16 |
| A TURN IS HEARD WHEN SOMEBODY COMES IN ON IT: if the last arrival of a song is its weakest, list who enters at every other boundary and who enters at this one. If the answer is nobody (everything was in already), do not add a part: make one that came in earlier WAIT, and choose the one the earlier boundary was made of (Least Common: the hat was made to wait for the last page and the curve did not move: the earlier turn was the pings', not the hat's: built, not shipped). And a chord is only turned over when its lowest voice moves: if that voice is the drum, tune the drum (a machine's bass drum follows its note) | take the part out of the whole section before, pickup and all, and keep its first stroke after the boundary exactly where it was; count the strokes taken (none may be added). Read the boundary on the novelty curve with each change alone: the drum's note alone did not move it | chirper-one, Least Common v17 |
| A MOTOR, NOT A SIDECHAIN: to make a ringing thing breathe with the beat, turn its own level with an LFO a beat long that starts again with each stroke (`retrig`) at its top: the stroke keeps its level and the ring dips half a beat later | it is in the patch, so every copy of the stroke (the dry one, the one sent to the hall) turns alike, and it needs no drum: it keeps the beat where the drum rests. Read the finished song in the thing's band on the beat against the off-beat, section by section, and read what the ring lost on average (a sine in dB of depth d costs d/2) | chirper-one, Ring True v17 |
| VOICE THE ARRIVAL WHERE THE TENSION WAS: if a part is what makes a section tense (a sub under a bass on the fifth), do not end the section by taking it away: let it play the first bar of the resolution (the tonic's own note in the same voice) and take it away a bar later.
