And let the drum make the same journey the other way (tuned up a fifth while the bass is down a fourth), so that both fall onto the key note on one stroke | four notes and no new sound: the sub's last bar is on the home note; the drum's notes change for the tense bars only. Read the bar of arrival against the bars after it (whole, and in the sub's band): if it stands more than a dB or two over them it is a boom, not an arrival | chirper-one, Wind Down v17 |
| LET AN ARRIVAL STAND BEFORE ITS LINE MOVES: a section that arrives on the home note after a section away must stay on it long enough to be an arrival. A bass that began to turn (G D C by bar) in the peak's first bar cost the peak its boundary; the same notes from the peak's ninth bar cost nothing | a `from` bar on the rule (`FROM = {section: 8}`). Read the scorer's curve at the section's first bar with the rule from bar 0 and from the half before publishing. And an octave copy of a low line (9 dB under) is 4 to 5 dB more in the octave's band in the finished song (0.6 raw), nothing two octaves up, and costs the whole song 0.3 dB: it serves a speaker that reaches the octave, not one that begins above it | chirper-one, Small Change v17 |
| READ WHERE THE PEAK IS ON THE TURNS THAT WORK: a turn is heard where a held, pitched thing comes into an EMPTY band, and that may be before the turn (the bass's lead note in a bare break), not on it. Give a weak turn the same emptiness before it, not a new part after it | print the scorer's novelty peaks beside the form's marks; the strongest turn's peak was 13 sixteenths before the turn, on the bass's held note. Emptying 7.5 beats of the low band before the weak turn took it from 0.12 to 0.23; moving a bright part (the pings) to arrive on it gave 0.01. Two costs to read: a new section that is like its neighbour in timbre LOWERS the variety term (2 points here, more than structure gave back); and a quiet held pedal under half a song costs timbre spread and recurrence (0.8 to 1.1 of the total at -28 dB) | chirper-one, Least Common v18 |
| TWO RINGS THAT TAKE TURNS: give the thing struck ON the beat and the thing struck OFF it the same disc (an LFO a beat long on the ring's level, starting again at each stroke at its top): each ring is then down where the other is struck | read the finished song by section in each thing's band, the 120 ms that begin on the off-beats against those on the beats; and read the band itself: a ring with 12 dB dips is about 4 dB less energy, and a low ring's loss is the section's (the master gives the top the room: +0.9 dB above 2.5 kHz in the section that lost 2.4 dB below 120 Hz). Bench the LFO on the layer TYPE first: it is exact on a modal layer and does nearly nothing on a noise layer | chirper-one, Ring True v18 |
| LET THE FORM RING ITS OWN QUARTERS: if a song divides in four, a clock can say so: one change at the first quarter, two at the half, three at the third, four at the end, on the clock's own seconds and not the song's beat | render each use alone: a tonal part alone in a sparse ending costs the song's timbre spread and recurrence (the ending stops being unlike the rest) and makes that passage louder: trim it there (8 dB here) and read the passage's level; read every note over the music in a band round its own tone (chirper-zero's measure) and set the fader in the finished song, not raw | chirper-one, Wind Down v18 (tune: chirper-zero's QUART_CHANGES) |
| A RISER MADE BY A TABLE: a spun coin's rattle, written from its law: the rate rises as (t0 - t)^(-1/3), so click k of N is at T (1 - (1 - k/N)^(3/2)) and N is one and a half times the clicks a steady rate would give; begin it at the song's own grid rate so it is first heard as the grid; two tracks by turns with a pan lane closing as (t0 - t)^(2/3); it must END on a silence or an arrival | bench it alone: every written click found to 0.1 ms; left less right by click (5 dB closing to 0); 100 dB down 30 ms after t0. In the finished song read the boundary it ends on (here 0.41 to 0.56 where a drum comes in, 0.41 to 0.46 where a band leaves) and its band by bar, new for old: over a full band it stood 2 to 5 dB over the hats for three bars and 10 in the last | chirper-one, Small Change v18 |
| COUNT A PAGE IN TWO WITH TWO DRUM NOTES, NOT WITH A REST: every second stroke on another note (here a third under: the page before's) gives the two without emptying the low band; half time (the drum resting) empties it and the spectrum term, a mean of dB over frames, charges every empty frame in a band that is already short | read the scorer's variety by its three parts before naming a cause: `tools/compscore.py` m_variety is level range, timbre spread AND the spread of strong-onset density between sections (wanted 0.2 of the mean): print the sections' densities at each cut (dens.py). In the finished song read the two notes' bands on each kind of beat: here the lower band less the upper is -16 dB on the page's note and -4.5 on the other | chirper-one, Least Common v19 |
| FLUTTER-TONGUE A BREATH WITH A TREMOLO ON ITS TRACK, ITS RATE A NOTE LENGTH: `fx: [{type: tremolo, depth: 0.85, rate: '1/48'}]` on a track that holds only the strokes to be rolled (the others stay on the part's own track; give the new track the same patch, pan and sends, and add it to every bus `returns` that heard the old one). An LFO on a `noise` layer's level does not do this (Ring True v18's bench) | bench one breath alone first, ON THE SAME TRACK with and without the effect (a breath is noise and a new track is a new noise: two tracks compared read 0.2 to 0.7 dB at any rate, with nothing there): the level from 500 to 6000 Hz over 5 ms windows, new less old: 0.7 deep is 10.4 dB down at each roll, regular through the breath's 320 ms; after it only the room's tail is left, and that is not rolled. A free rate in Hz reads what a note length reads (CORRECTED 16:06: this row first said it was smeared: wrong); the note length is for the phase: every stroke on a beat meets the roll open at any tempo. In the finished song read each rolled stroke in a band round its own note against the same bars with no roll: other parts fill the gaps (here 2.7 to 3.1 dB up and down for 5.0 alone, and 1.0 with no roll at all) | chirper-one, Ring True v19 |
| LET A TUNED STRING HEAR A BELL THAT IS ITS OWN NOTE: a delay loop a period long (`returns: [{from: BELL, gain: -40dB, time: PERIOD}, {from: itself, gain: -0.13dB, time: PERIOD}]`) on a bell whose partials are the string's harmonics: the string keeps the partials the bell lets die (here the fifth: 5 to 13 dB more from the second second on) and moves the bell toward the string's side | the feed is small because the loop multiplies what is in tune with it (-0.13 dB a pass is about 36 dB at the note): bench the bell alone with and without, by second and by ear side (left less right): at -34 dB the bell's second second crossed to the string's side; at -46 half of -40's | chirper-one, Wind Down v19 |
| DAMP A RING BY THE GAP TO THE NEXT STROKE: where strokes quicken (a spun coin, a bounce, a roll), put each stroke's ring on the note (`expr: {ring: seconds}` into the modal layer's `decay`) and write it as a fixed number of gaps (here two, as T60): the ring is then the same share of every gap, long while the strokes are far apart and choked as they close up, and the last stroke is the shortest: it stops dead | read the second half of each gap against that stroke's first 6 ms, by quarter of the run (here 20.5 19.8 18.3 16.2 dB under: even; one fixed 60 ms ring read 62 52 41 25: nothing at first and a smear at the end), and the 30 ms after the stop against the 30 ms before (37 dB for 13). Its cost: the last strokes are smaller than a smear's: the run's last instant read 3 to 4 dB quieter | chirper-one, Small Change v19 |
| A PEDAL THAT STRIKES THE SAME NOTE AGAIN NEEDS A BREATH: a held bass note written right up to the next stroke of the same note is one long note: let each go a little before the next (here two sixteenths, on a sine with a 60 ms release) | read the note's band in the 150 ms before each stroke against the 400 ms after it: held right up, D2 read -16.0 dBFS before and -16.6 after (no stroke); with the breath -41.9 before and -16.5 after. And read the two halves of the beat in 60 to 130 Hz: a pedal sits UNDER the drums (the half beat from the drum 1.4 dB over the half before the next) where a cell of short notes sat between them (1.6 dB the other way) | chirper-one, Least Common v20 |
| TRADE CELLS, NOT SOUNDS: to move a tune from one thing to another without the form terms moving, give each thing the other's cell (its instants, notes and hardness), an octave over if their registers differ; walk a drum's note down on a `modal` kettle head and give each lower step its level back on the stroke | read each thing's band in the 60 ms from the two cells' instants, before and after: the louder instant moves to the other's place (here 2.5 dB for the wood, 6.6 for the metal, the other way round before). The scorer saw nothing: no new turn, recurrence 0.335 for 0.333. The kettle head alone read 0.7, 0.4 and 1.8 dB under B flat 1 on G1, F1 and D1 (D1's own band 3.8 under): 0.5, 0.5 and 2 dB on the strokes made the bars level. Three bars at 37 to 49 Hz moved the song's shortest band 0.2 dB | chirper-one, Ring True v20 |
| A LOCKED GROOVE (the end of a record's side): what says 'record' and not 'clock' is that EVERYTHING comes round the same: give the turn a length (1.8 s at 33 1/3), put a click at the joint and a few specks at fixed places in the turn, and make each of them, and the surface under them, from noise that is the same at every note-on (chip noise with `clock: 0`), the surface as one note a turn at a step rate too slow to repeat inside the turn. Start the turn at a phase that keeps clear of anything else that ticks. End it by lifting the needle: a low thump and the surface cut, not faded | read turn against turn after everything else has stopped: the correlation of the two turns' samples and their difference under a turn (here 1.000 and 37 dB above 600 Hz; with one held white hiss 0.52 and 0.2 dB). The scorer counted the run-out as a section once it had a surface or an end (structure 100 for 99.33); the clicks alone, in a longer song, read under | chirper-one, Wind Down v20 |
| COUNT WHAT A DELAY PAIR CAN PAY (the coin problem as a hat): where a note goes into two delays that feed each other (p and q sixteenths), put a short click on every amount a p + b q after it: scattered at first, and from (p q - p - q) + 1 on EVERY sixteenth: a line that becomes a run by arithmetic. Give each click its place by how it is made (a alone on the first delay's side, b alone on the second's, both in the middle) and take a fixed number of dB off for each coin (a + b), as the delays do; count further each time; strike p q, the first amount made two ways, on both sides | bench the line alone against whatever else is in those bars (a break is lifted: here 17 dB: a click benched at -32 dBFS arrived at -10 to -14) and read the click's highest sample at each amount against the amounts that cannot be made (here -10 to -26 against -55). A part put into the bars after a turn softens the turn (0.33 for 0.38; 0.20 for 0.25; 0.42 for 0.49) | chirper-one, Small Change v20 |
| LET THE DRUM TAKE A RESTING PART'S CYCLE (the floor moves, nothing changes speed): where one voice of a polyrhythm rests, write the bass drum on that voice's instants in place of the beat, on that voice's note, and let the voice's first two strokes be struck with it 6 and 12 dB down (and its last two come back): the part is heard to change hands. Thin what else says the beat as the drum slows (the hat on every off beat, every second, none) and bring it all back at once | bench the drum alone at each note (here G1, B flat 1, D2 within 0.7 dB at their highest, but a stroke on D2 about 3 dB poorer under 90 Hz than one on G1). To the scorer the drum's strokes are not the weight (35 for 13 moved its strong strokes a second by 0.5); the hat's are (40 fewer: 0.9, and the variety term 3 up) | chirper-one, Least Common v21 |
| TUNE A PAIR APART BY THE TEMPO (a ring that ticks with no gate): strike a second bar with the first, BPM x 4 / 60 Hz over it for a breath a sixteenth long (half that for an eighth, a quarter for a beat), both in one place and each 6 dB under the single stroke: in step at the stroke, cancelled half a wave later, in step again on the grid. THE ENGINE'S MODAL PARTS WANDER: each stroke sounds 2 cents under to 5 over its note (`vary`, on by default, per layer), so give both bars `vary: 0` or the breath is anywhere from half to one and a half times what was written | bench one note eight times and read each stroke's own frequency and the minima of its envelope before believing a gap; in a finished song the dips after the first are filled by whatever else is in the band (the hall's copy of one bar, the room, the stroke before) | chirper-one, Ring True v21 |
| A LANE ON A FILTER'S CUTOFF IS IN OCTAVES, AND A HELD NOTE NEEDS `gate: 1`: `hp cutoff=700+auto.slide*-1.276` with the lane at 1 is 700 x 2^-1.276 = 289 Hz; written in Hz (`*-411`) the cutoff goes to nothing and the track is SILENT, and `chirp plan` says nothing. A note is let go at 95% of its written length (`play.gate`): one note of 800 beats stopped 19 s early. A lane on a gain (`gain: {value: '-51dB', mod: [{src: auto.held, amount: 1}]}`) is in dB | render the part alone over its first and its last bars before the song, and read where it stops and its level by band | chirper-one, Wind Down v21 |
| MAKE A POLYRHYTHM ONE RHYTHM AND GIVE IT BACK (fusion by timbre): for a whole number of its common periods, strike every voice at every instant at which ANY voice falls (each 3 dB down), and rest whatever names their meetings; then give each voice its own strokes back at the next common meeting. No instant is added or moved: only whose stroke it is can no longer be told | read, in the band of the brightest voice, that every instant of the union has a stroke and no other instant does (here 57 of 57 and 0 of 48); this scorer finds the section but no difference in it (level, timbre and density as before) and takes about 0.1 from its variety term | chirper-one, Least Common v22 |
| A PAIR TUNED APART WANTS BOTH BARS IN ITS HALL, AND A HALL DOES NOT BREATHE: if only the first bar is sent to the hall, the hall holds that one steady tone under a pair that is cancelling, and fills the nulls with exactly what they took out. Send the hall both (each 6 dB under the single copy). The hall will not show the breath (it smears 60 ms): it stands 4 to 6 dB lower where the bars are a sixteenth apart, about 1 dB where they are a beat apart | read the hall alone with two bars less the hall with one, by 15 ms window after a stroke; in the finished song look for the SECOND dip in a band round the note (here 5.5 to 8 dB deeper), not for a row of them | chirper-one, Ring True v22 |
| A RECORD'S PRE-ECHO, AND A SILENCE THE SCORER LEANS ON: copy the first turn (1.8 s) of a loud entry into the bare bar before it, one turn early and 30 dB down, on tracks of their own (same patches, places, sends). In a bare bar this master lifts it about 14 dB: the drum's ghost stood 25 dB under its entry. But a bare bar whose cut is near the scorer's highest setting (0.18 to 0.20) loses the cut to ANY ghost (0.16 at 30 dB down, 0.18 at 38): 1.08 off the total for three such bars | read nov2.py's peak at each bare bar BEFORE filling it; fill only those with 0.05 to spare; a hiss under ticks shows in the floor between them (the quietest 100 ms), not in the level | chirper-one, Wind Down v22 |
| ROUNDING INSIDE A LOOP (a till with no small coins): the engine's `crush bits=5 rate=...` last in a feedback bus's effects rounds the echo again at every pass: under half a step is nothing, so the tail stops dead instead of fading; with a pitch effect and filters in the loop no smallest coin goes round for ever. `bits` is a plain number (no lane); `rate` takes a lane, in octaves; a rate over the engine's 48 kHz holds nothing; `mix` takes a lane | a level meter cannot see it (the part's level moved under 0.1 dB while what changed stood 10 dB under the part): read the one less the other, sample by sample, and the quietest 100 ms (the gaps empty) | chirper-one, Small Change v22 |
| A BREAK THAT KEEPS ITS FLOOR (a dominant pedal): take the drum and the bass's line out and let the bass hold ONE note under the break (the fifth; the drum comes back on the tonic), at the level the song's held leads have (-13 dB on the note here: it stood 10 to 11 dB under the groove's lows, the break 9 dB down whole). A held note counts nothing, so an uneven rhythm above it stays uncounted. Bare, the same break was 16 dB down and cost 0.10: the scorer averages each band in dB, and 12.5 s of nothing in the low band is 1.1 dB at 71 Hz | read the low band by half supercycle, the step in and out (2 s each side), and the scorer's worst band; the ticks came up only 2 dB when the drum left: no trim | chirper-one, Least Common v23 |
| IS A SOFT PART COVERED? (chirper-zero's reading, used on a part already in): render the finished song again with the part's notes taken out of the bars in question, and read each band's highest sample in the 40 ms from every sixteenth of the bar, as the median over the bars: with, without, the one less the other. Ring True's plate on beats 2 and 4 under the drum stood 8.0 and 4.3 dB over everything else in its notes' band and 24 to 27 dB in its click's: not covered; the weaker beat is named | tools: tops.py DIR WITH WITHOUT LO HI A B; a pair of bars tuned a BEAT's wave apart shows no dip in a finished song: it reads as one bar, and is the right last step before one | chirper-one, Ring True v23 |
| A SONG WITH NO TOP, AND WHERE A SMALL SOUND CAN LIVE: read the top octave less the whole by eight bars (Wind Down stood 44 to 50 dB under for 104 bars). Then read each band's highest sample by sixteenth of the bar on the old music: a 6 ms noise tooth stood +11.8 dB over it from 12.5 to 17.5 kHz and +7.5 from 9 to 12.5 kHz, and +0.2 from 5 to 9 kHz, where the click's own wood is: a sound put where its part already is cannot be heard as another sound. A band cut out of noise by a filter is no louder for it (the density is the same): lift the fader. And a LOW noise under sparse ticks fills the floor between them: it cost the scorer a section at -20 and at -32 dB; in the run-out alone it cost nothing | tools: top.py (the top by eight bars), tops.py DIR NEW OLD LO HI A B (a band by sixteenth, one render less another), low.py (the end by 2 s) | chirper-one, Wind Down v23 |
| WHICH OCTAVE FOR A NEW PART: read the part AS DELIVERED, not the sum: render the finished song with it and without it on one seed, take the one less the other sample by sample, band it, and read its highest sample by sixteenth against the old render's in the same band and bar. Equal parts on one sixteenth do not raise a sum's top (a sum read +0.0 where the part stood level with the music), so a reading of the sum says 'covered' of a part that is not. Small Change's key was tried at three octaves in three builds: under the drum (10 to 15 dB under it on the beat), on the seed (its first strokes the seed over again), an octave over (within 6 dB on the one) | tools: part.py DIR NEW OLD LO HI BAR...; under about -10 the difference is the master's, not the part; read a bar WITH the lead in it and one without: the same part was 13 under in one and 30 over in the next | chirper-one, Small Change v23 |
| A SLOW LAYER THAT COSTS NO SECTION: a part that enters a few bars after a section begins blurs the small cut the section's own first entry makes (Least Common: three strokes round a held tone's entry 8 s into the half took a cut that read 0.15 under the edge: 0.15 off the total). Begin the layer at its own first landmark instead (here bar 121, where two of its cycles first meet): the sections came back at every setting. And a held tone under four pages made them alike (v18, 0.8 to 1.1 off); the same count as short strokes cost 0.03 | read nov2.py's peaks within 15 s after every section head before adding a layer there; deliv.py DIR NEW OLD LO HI BEAT... reads each stroke as delivered, by window | chirper-one, Least Common v24 |
| A SLOW MODULATION ON A HELD NOTE MUST TURN AT A RATE THE DRUM DOES NOT HAVE: this master dips a held note at every stroke (Ring True's glass: 5.6 dB in the 60 ms from the drum, 2.4 at the metal's stroke, 3.8 at the wood's), so the note already rocks once a beat. A tremolo once a beat at 3 dB was 1.9 dB of that and a build without it scored the same to the thousandth. Two turns to three beats and 6 dB reads: the second half of each turn 4 to 5 dB lower than without it | fold.py DIR NAME LO HI T0 T1 [BEATS]: fold the part as delivered (the build less the old one) on the beat and on the modulation's own turn, with it and without it | chirper-one, Ring True v24 |
| PLACE BY THE TEMPO MAP AS WRITTEN, NOT BY THE CURVE IT FOLLOWS: Wind Down's wind-down is a platter's curve written as a tempo ramp a beat; the curve and the map are 9 ms apart at the last click (31 bpm). A tooth placed in seconds by the curve against a click placed in beats was 10 ms from where it was meant, and a reader with a 12 ms window missed it. Turn the map's own function round (beat_at by halving: second_at) for anything in seconds set against something in beats | teeth.py DIR SONG PART OLD T0BEAT: each tooth against its click and the old music; and an LFO with no retrig runs on song time: lock it to a landmark with its phase | chirper-one, Wind Down v24 |
| THE FIRST SOUND AFTER HALF A BAR OF NOTHING IN ITS BAND COMES OUT WITH THE LEAD: Small Change's key off the beat: a payment's first stroke where no seed is sown read -6.5 dBFS, the seed's own level, and the same payment's other strokes -12 to -21 (the same patch, 1.9 dB of touch between them). Trim by PLACE, not by part: 9 dB off those strokes only put them among their neighbours (-15.5); the same stroke with a seed under it was 7.8 dB under the seed and wanted nothing | render the song once WITHOUT the part and read every build against it: strokes.py DIR SONG PART OLD T0BEAT LO HI BAR... prints each stroke's top as delivered and what is under it | chirper-one, Small Change v24 |
| AN ECHO THAT GOES ROUND IS AS MANY STROKES A SECOND AS THE MUSIC IT REPLACED; ONE ECHO IS A FIGURE: Least Common's one click at the large size: 19 strokes in 16.7 s with the feedback echo the clicks already had (3 6 9, 5 10, 7 14 sixteenths) read 5.9 strokes a second between pages of 6.7 and 6.3, and was no break at all; the same strokes with a single echo each read 3.9 where the scorer cut | put the strokes on a track of their own (a copy of the part's track, same patch) and give it a bus with ONE return and no return from itself: `apart-x: {returns: [{from: one-x, gain: -4dB, time: 357.14ms}]}`; the old track keeps its own bus | chirper-one, Least Common v25 |
| TWO MILLISECONDS OF NOISE ARE NOT A CLICK YOU CAN COUNT ON: Ring True's first notch was noise alone through a narrow band (q 4 at 2.3 kHz: about 600 Hz wide, so 2 ms hold two or three independent values): the same click read -11 to -23 dBFS from bar to bar, and some notches of a row were missing. A very short modal bar (6 ms) with the noise on top as grit reads the same to 0.1 dB every time | for a row of equal clicks use a struck model for the body and noise only as colour, 15 to 20 dB under it; bench the same click in three bars before trusting it | chirper-one, Ring True v25 |
| A ROW THAT FOLLOWS A DRUM STROKE DOES NOT FALL AWAY UNLESS THE FALL IS WRITTEN: Wind Down's tally, a click on the beat and six after it 30 ms apart at one level and a falling touch, came out -13, then -22 -18 -17 -18 -19 -19 dBFS: this master dips what falls in the 30 ms after the drum (+4 to +8 dB over the bench there) and lifts what follows more the farther it is from the drum (+8 rising to +13) | read one finished build click by click, then write the shape in: the first after the drum 3 dB up, each later one 1.5 dB under the one before gave -13, then -17 -20 -21 -22 -24 -28 | chirper-one, Wind Down v25 |
| A GRACE NOTE BEFORE A DRUM STROKE COMES OUT AS LOUD AS THE NOTE IT LEADS TO: Small Change's clasp, a click 30 ms before the beat written 6 dB under the click on the beat, came out level with it or over it (-10.7 to -14.4 dBFS against -11.5 to -14.4): this master lifts a 20 ms click 21 to 22 dB when it falls 30 ms before the drum and 10 to 12 when it falls on it; on a beat the drum has left, 15 to 21 | write the grace note 8 dB further down than the ear would (14 under gave 6 to 8 under), and trim the same part by place where the drum does not play (10 dB off on beat 2 and 7 on beat 4 gave -12.6 and -13.4 to -15.9) | chirper-one, Small Change v25 |
| THE SAME NOTE ON SEVENTEEN DRUM STROKES IS SEVENTEEN LEVELS; AND A MODAL BAR CAN BE TUNED BY A FRACTION: Least Common's eleven, a 260 ms bar at 539 Hz struck on a beat every eleven beats at one level, came out -12.4 to -21.1 dBFS (raw and alone -20.7 every time): 8.7 dB by where in the bar it falls and what is ringing there | read one finished build note by note and give each note the trim that brings it to the aim: the trims came out whole (1.5 dB between the seventeen; 3.1 on the other seed). For a note outside the scale: a modal layer honours `ratio` (1.375 on G4 read 538.9 Hz) and ignores `detune` | chirper-one, Least Common v26 |
| READ THE OLD SONG BY SIXTEENTH IN A NEW PART'S BAND BEFORE CHOOSING WHERE ITS PHRASE BEGINS: Ring True's comb (175 to 466 Hz), begun on the one, had its first note 8 to 11 dB under the weight there after every trim; the finished song without it read -5 to -7 dBFS on the four beats and -28 to -34 on sixteenth 6 | begin the phrase on the emptiest sixteenth (its first note came out 12 to 21 dB over what is there), then trim each place from one finished reading: the master lifts the emptiest place most (15 to 16 dB against 1.6 on a beat at the peak), and a cut of more than 4 dB wants writing 1.3 times as deep (24 places 12.4 dB apart became 1.8) | chirper-one, Ring True v26 |
| A SHORT NOTE IN A HALL IS HEARD AT THE HALL'S PITCH, NOT ITS OWN: Wind Down's eleven (20 dB down in 70 ms) sent to the hall at -14 dB read, over 0.3 s from each stroke, 277, 282, 288 or 299 Hz for 269.5 to 294 written: up to 43 cents off, and the last, meant to land in tune, 33 cents sharp. And READ THE SONG BY BAND BEFORE CHOOSING A NEW PART'S OCTAVE: at 547 Hz the song was already 5.8 dB over the album's envelope (tolerance 6) | keep a short note that must be in tune dry (it read within 4 cents; the score, which had fallen 0.03 on the hall's tail, came back to 0.002 under), and read the pitch of anything that must be in tune AS DELIVERED; put a new part where `compscore --bands` shows room (here an octave down: 250 and 292 Hz were 0.5 under) | chirper-one, Wind Down v26 |
| A HELD TONE IN A SONG OF CLICKS UNDER A LOUDNESS TARGET IS AT ONCE THE LOUDEST THING IN IT, AND ITS FADER TURNS THE REST OF THE SONG UP: Small Change's product (two plain tones, half a bar, 24 times) at -16 dB came out 3 to 10 dB over the drum and bass; at -24 dB it came out only 0.8 to 2.8 dB lower and both ends of the song 0.8 dB lower | read BOTH ENDS with every build of a held part; set its level by what else is in its band as delivered (here -38 dB put it 3 to 5 dB under the drum and bass and the ends back within 0.1 dB); expect 21 to 23 dB of lift over the bench. Ring modulation in a patch: an osc layer's `ringmod: {from, mix}` from a silent earlier layer is exact (the carrier 75 to 115 dB down at mix 1) | chirper-one, Small Change v26 |
| A CUT THAT SITS ON A THRESHOLD CAN BE LOST TO A PART THAT BEGINS 55 s AFTER IT: Least Common's cut at 3:28 is printed 0.15 high in v26 and in every build of v27, whose new part begins at 4:23; it stopped being a cut for one or two of the seven thresholds on BOTH seeds (0.12 of score), with the render there unchanged (-74 dBFS between them) | read nov2.py's peaks against the thresholds before a round and name the ones that sit on one; if such a cut is lost, say so and do not chase it: here moving the part's first stroke 11 bars and leaving its last out each left the score the same to 0.002 | chirper-one, Least Common v27 |
| RING THE CHANGES ON A TUNE THAT REPEATS: n notes in a fixed order, bar after bar, are rounds; the plain hunt (every bar each note changes places with a neighbour, by turns the pairs from the first place and the pairs from the second) gives 2n different bars and is back in rounds on the next: Ring True's comb, seven tongues for fourteen bars. The same notes at the same instants in another order cost the scorer nothing (0.001 to 0.005; the same sections) | keep the rhythm and the trims by PLACE (raw, the tongues were within 2.1 dB of each other in nine places of ten, and 3.0 in the tenth) and move only the pitches; mark one note (the treble) a few dB up so the pattern can be followed; time a course to come round on a landmark; then read the part note by note: the same place ranged 12 dB from bar to bar by what it fell on | chirper-one, Ring True v27 |
| THE HALL HAS NOTES OF ITS OWN (the album's hall: `reverb kind=hall decay=3.2s`, high-passed at 350 Hz): a wooden bar that rings 350 ms, sent there at -12 dB at nine pitches from 269.5 to 294 Hz, kept its pitch for its first half second (within 8 cents) and a second later the hall was sounding 277 Hz or 297.5 Hz whatever had been written (up to 47 cents off); the stroke written nearest 277 rang on for a second, 6 to 18 dB over the others' answers | read what is left 0.6 to 1.6 s after each stroke (pitch and level) before sending a low or moving pitch to the hall; keep a part whose pitch moves dry; a long note is levelled by its ring (eight strokes within 0.9 dB with no trims), a short one by its top | chirper-one, Wind Down v27 |
| TO FILL A BAND THE SONG IS SHORT IN, HOLD A NOTE THERE; TO KEEP IT FROM OVERFILLING, HOLD FEWER, NOT SOFTER: the scorer's reading by band follows how long a band is filled, not how loud (it averages each band in dB). Small Change was 2 to 4.6 dB under the album from 44 to 133 Hz with a bass of sixteenth notes; one note a bar held 5 to 7 sixteenths put it 2 to 3 dB OVER; 6 dB off those notes moved that 0.5 dB; holding them in every second bar put the lows on the album (within 2.1 dB) | read compscore --bands, recurrence and both ends with each build: the long note and the short one that steps back after it, in every bar, also made bars alike (recurrence 0.571 to 0.676) and, through the loudness target, lowered both ends 1.2 dB (0.5 with the long notes 6 dB down); in every second bar, 0.571 and 0.24 dB: and of that 0.24 the 68 held notes are 0.04 and the 47 SHORT notes at full level 0.20 (rendered apart, both seeds): a short low note at full level costs the ends more than a held one 6 dB under it. A bell on the drum (4 dB at 71 Hz) bought 0.5 dB in that band for 0.6 dB off both ends: left off | chirper-one, Small Change v27 |
| END A NEW PART WHERE THE SONG ALREADY CHANGES: a part that plays on across a cut lowers that cut. Least Common's big brothers as one click, carried through the bar where the bass, the hat and the clap leave, took the scorer's peak there from 0.14 to 0.13 and a section with it on both seeds (0.115 / 0.057 lower) and raised the first bar of the seam 0.44 dB; ended at that bar the peak was 0.14 again and the seam as it was to 0.01 dB | name the cuts a new part crosses (nov2.py) before writing it, and end it on one; and READ WHAT IS ON AN INSTANT BEFORE LENDING A SOUND TO IT: a tom lent to 9 bar lines that have the bass drum on them came out 0.7 dB over the drum alone in its band (raw, 3.4 dB under it): not heard, where a wooden bar lent to the same bar lines is 6.2 dB over what was there | chirper-one, Least Common v28 |
| A WALK ACROSS THE STEREO ON TWO TRACKS: the same note on a track at 0.6 left and one at 0.6 right, shared by equal power (cos and sin of the place): 7.0 dB between the sides at each end, none in the middle; but the two sides TOGETHER, as one speaker and the scorer hear them, are 2.6 to 3.0 dB under the middle at the ends | write the ends up (1.5 dB here gave -1.5 for one speaker and +1.5 for two); and count what the part costs elsewhere: in a loudness-targeted song a new part in one band is paid for a little in the others, and a worst band can sit on a step (here 38 Hz: 6.5 dB short read 99.54 of spectrum and 6.6 short 99.46: 0.009 of score for a tenth of a dB; taking either of two small things off the gourd crossed it back) | chirper-one, Ring True v28 |
| A ROW OF 6 ms CLICKS NEEDS NO TRIMS, A SOFT RINGING NOTE DOES: Wind Down's notched stick was written into six new bars from bar 17 to bar 97 at the level it has from bar 145 on, and came out within 3.7 dB of every other row though the master lifts the first one 13.5 dB; five soft strokes of a 900 ms bar at one level came out 4.5 to 4.8 dB over the music on three beats and 1.7 to 2.1 on two, where the music has a note a quarter-tone off in the same band (C4 or D4 beside 269.5 Hz) | read the strongest line in the band at each stroke as well as the level: where it is not the new note's, the note is under something; 3 dB written on those two gave 2.9 and 3.5 over. And a record's pre-echo copies whatever is written in the bar it echoes: a new part there makes a new track 30 dB down | chirper-one, Wind Down v28 |
